Advances in Digital Technology and Data-Driven Business Practices

**Series Editor: Nripendra Singh, Pennsylvania Western University, USA**

Digital technology in marketing is dynamic and continually evolving, providing marketers with innovative tools and strategies to connect with audiences in an increasingly digital-centric world. Adopting and adapting to these technologies is essential for staying competitive and effectively reaching target audiences. Businesses that leverage digital marketing effectively are better positioned to connect with their audience, drive business growth and stay competitive in the digital age. Beyond the technical and ethical, *Advances in Digital Technology and Data-Driven Business Practices* exhibits real-world applications of digital marketing and data-driven practices. Through compelling case studies, it demonstrates its efficacy in areas such as market research, competitor scrutiny and gleaning customer insights, traversing various channels, encompassing the likes of social media, e-commerce and email marketing.

About the Editors

**Dr Babita Singla** is a Professor at Chitkara Business School, Chitkara University, Punjab, India. She has a PhD in Management and is UGC-NET qualified. She has over 14 years of experience in teaching, research and administration. Her areas of expertise are marketing, e-commerce, omnichannel and retail. In her career, she has been involved in important academic and research assignments such as *Guest Editor of a reputed Journal*, *organising and conducting International and National Level Conferences, Faculty Development Programs*, and a guide for the research projects along her Teaching. Her overall research-oriented mindset, rich knowledge and dedication towards her responsibilities have awarded her with the most prestigious *Teaching Excellence Award* and *Outstanding Achievement Award*. She has more than 70 research publications in reputable international and national journals, including 42 indexed in *Scopus, Web of Science and SCI*, and has presented research papers at various national and international conferences. In the short span of her career in Academia and Administration, she has authored and edited several books on retail, supply chain, branding, customer relationship management and product management; covering the course content of various universities *nationwide with reputed publication houses including Taylor & Francis, Springer, Emerald and IGI Global among others*. She is a lifetime member of various organisations of national and international repute. She has successfully delivered guest sessions at international and national universities. Other notable achievements are the registered *Copyrights and Patents* in her name.

**Kumar Shalender** (Post Doctorate, PhD) holds expertise in the fields of strategic marketing, leadership change, and technology management. He is an Associate Professor at Chitkara Business School, Chitkara University, Punjab, and has been involved in devising corporate strategy, business models and sustainability ecosystems. He has more than 80 publications to his credit, including 49 papers in highly reputed Scopus-indexed journals, international and national conferences, seminars and symposiums. Dr Kumar also holds authority on solving intricacies of design thinking, innovation culture, collaborative practices and team dynamics. He is associated with leading publications in the sustainability space and is having regular interactions with professionals on developing sustainable ecosystems across industries in the country.

**Dr Nripendra Singh** is a tenured Full Professor of Marketing in the Department of Business, Economics and Communication, College of Science, Technology and Business at the Pennsylvania Western University. He is a recipient of Fulbright scholarship, a flagship programme of the United States Department of State, Bureau of Educational and Cultural Affairs. He completed Fulbright research at the Pennsylvania State University. As a visiting research scholar, Dr Singh visited renowned institutions such as University of California at Berkley, CA, and Technology University, NJ. He is a Post-Doc Fellow of the Global Institute of Flexible Systems and Technology and ‘Campus-2-Capitol Fellow’ of Bard College, NY. He earned two doctorate degrees and two MBA degrees. As a certified professional, he completed the Preparing Future Faculty programme at the Iowa State University. Dr Singh is an avid researcher and is on the editorial board and reviewers committee of several reputed journals. He has published and presented more than 100 research articles in reputed journals and conferences. He is the President and Director of ‘International Association of Technology and Management’, a nonprofit to promote research, and ‘Clarion Consulting Center’ to provide consulting services to businesses and help students get real-world projects. He mentors and advises young faculty members, research scholars and undergrad students globally.

Preface

The combined use of data science (DS) and artificial intelligence (AI) is increasing by the day. Businesses across the industry vertical are using these technologies to explore new opportunities in addition to strengthening their current market positions. *Navigating Data Science: Unleashing the Creative Potential of Artificial Intelligence* takes a comprehensive look into the combined capability of DS and AI with specific reference to real-world business scenarios. From analysing the use of New Age innovations in marketing to science and technology and the healthcare sector, the authors have contributed to enhance the body of knowledge in the field of DS and AI. The ethical considerations of using AI tools in education, academic research and gig workers are also discussed. The impact of technology in moulding the overall well-being of employees within the specific framework of their work life has been discussed by the authors. Among the key highlights of the title is the comprehensive and holistic approach that the authors have taken while contributing chapters. The global perspective enhances the usability and relevance of the book for a wider set of audiences. Especially in relation to the science and technology domain, the use of the DS and AI will encourage stakeholders to come together and contribute to harness the potential of these technologies on a larger scale. The ethical concerns related to the use of AI are also analysed. This is critical for the field of propagating AI as the use of the technology has been surrounded by myriad issues with policymakers across the globe debating very passionately about setting a framework for the use of AI. The discussion and implications offered by this book can prove instrumental in finally developing an AI framework that will lead to the right use of the technology within the set boundaries. The implications of using big data to generate information from AI are also part of the contents covered by the book. The chapter on using the DS and AI in the healthcare sector also features some crucial implication for the policymakers. As the role of technology is very crucial in offering quality and affordable healthcare services, the chapter will help industry stakeholders deliberate on the use of technology for benefitting patients. It is essential to note that taking things forward in the technology domain, especially in relation to the DS and AI, stakeholders need to come together and collaborate in a constructive manner. This approach will help to reduce the burden of the resources to develop new use case of these technologies that, in turn, will lead to widespread adoption of these innovations at a mass level. Summarily, this book is a valuable addition to the knowledge body and will help academics, professionals and policymakers.

## Chapter 1

## Data Science and Artificial Intelligence: Exploring Collaborative Potential for Healthcare Sector

Kumar Shalender^a, Babita Singla^a and Nripendra Singh^b

^a Chitkara Business School, Chitkara University, Punjab, India

^b Pennsylvania Western University, USA

### Abstract

Exploring the game-changing potential of the data science and artificial intelligence (AI) in the healthcare sector, this chapter specifically looks into the use cases to develop practical guidelines for the industry to implement the technology at a higher level. By analysing the use cases of data science and AI in the healthcare industry, the chapter identifies key areas where the combined use of these technologies is bringing superior benefits to the stakeholders. These cases then become a base for the development of practical guidelines that can be adopted by the healthcare industry to adopt, integrate and intensify the use of technology in relevant areas of business performance. The chapter concludes with the fact that adoption of the holistic approach is required for the long-term adoption of the data science and AI in the healthcare sector. The study also emphasises close collaboration between industry and policymakers so that the process of technology is adoption can be sustained for a longer period of time.

*Keywords*: Artificial intelligence (AI); collaboration; data science; healthcare sector; policymakers; stakeholders

### 1\. Introduction

The transformative potential of data science and artificial intelligence (AI) has just begun to find mention across the academic world and corporate sectors. Technology is enabling businesses to make sweeping changes in their products and services and helping them to deliver superior value to the target market. From the automobile industry to the FMCG sector and the consumer durable industry to exports, the use of data science and AI is opening new avenues of growth and opportunities for the industry players. Against this specific background, this chapter explores the transformative potential of data science and AI for the healthcare industry. The healthcare sector is very important not only in terms of its contribution to the economy but also in ensuring that the masses continue to receive quality healthcare services at affordable prices. There is no doubt about the role of technology in guaranteeing that quality healthcare services can be offered to people without charging very high prices. Data science and AI has a great potential that can be realised by the healthcare sector, and by integrating the technology in its functional department as well as operational mechanisms, the sector can utilise the technology to achieve new heights of growth and profitability. Another important facet of data science and AI is its crucial role in catalysing innovation for the industry. Technology can be used to come up with different kinds of products and services that can help the entire healthcare sector to come up with new methods and mechanisms for delivering superior healthcare services to patients. Such is the large scope of applications related to generative intelligence in the healthcare sector that businesses across the verticals are now investing in data science and AI to come up with products specifically related to healthcare management ([Aggarwal et al., 2020](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib1)). The chapter is organised into the following sections: the use cases related to data science and AI in the healthcare sector have been discussed that is followed by the development of the conceptual framework that can help the industry holders to use the technology in healthcare practices. The Development of the Framework is then followed by the Conclusion and Discussion while the authors also detail the important implications related to the use of data science and AI in the healthcare industry. The study has important implications for all the stakeholders in the healthcare ecosystem, and by focusing on the specific benefits related to the value chain of the industry, this chapter will set the tone for future research ([Bisam, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib2)). Researchers and academicians are likely to benefit greatly from the study and can utilise the chapter in order to come up with innovative practices for making sure that they deliver superior patient care services to the masses. The research will also contribute to the cause of making superior healthcare services available to the masses and by implementing and integrating data science and AI in the day-to-day working of the healthcare industry; the significant benefits can be reaped by the concerned stakeholders.

### 2\. Data Science and AI in Healthcare: Use Cases

#### 2.1 Screening Patients

Data Science and AI is increasingly finding used in screening of the patients in hospitals. The technology is proving very successful and instrumental in reducing the load of the practitioners so that they can strategically invest their time in taking care of the patients. With the help of generative AI, the various images related to the medical records of the patients can be analysed quickly, and accordingly, the data can be forwarded to the doctors for further analysis and treatment procedures ([Bughin et al., 2017](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib3)). The fast completion of the preliminary process is of critical importance as it can help the doctors and practices to deliver the patient care services without any kind of unnecessary delay. It is also important to understand that the inclusion of the technology has also made the process more cost-efficient which means the vision of delivering affordable healthcare services to all can be realised quickly and efficiently. Another significant benefit of using technology in screening patients is the fast processing as unlike human beings that technology do not require any kind of a break or rest period. This means patients can be continuously screened by the technology, and accordingly, analysis is offered to the doctors for taking the remedial actions. There are many machines to which the integration of data science and AI can be done, and satisfactory results on the Diagnostic front can be achieved. The potential of making errors also happens to be quite low in the case of data science and AI, and by ensuring that the right kind of data is fed into the machine, the optimum results can be obtained from the technology ([Dhiman et al., 2018](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib4)). More and more hospitals are now investing in data science and AI to come up with the right kind of a diagnostic techniques and mechanisms, and it is just a matter of time before we start witnessing that technology becomes a means of operations related to patient diagnostics and treatment procedures. By simultaneously increasing the efficiency and effectiveness of diagnostic procedures, the use of data science and AI can also play a crucial role in furthering the concept of affordable healthcare in the world. Especially when it comes to the developing and under-developed economies, the role of technology in providing good healthcare services is very crucial. These countries require a proper investment in the healthcare infrastructure, and by using the technology, the cost efficiency related to the healthcare management practices, healthcare services and allied issues can also be tackled effectively. The role of policymakers becomes also very important in encouraging the use of technology in the healthcare industry. By promoting new investment in the sector and encouraging technology developers, entrepreneurs and allied players in the ecosystem, renewed progress can be achieved in the healthcare industry ([Dorn, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib5)).

#### 2.2 Vaccine Discovery

The large number of iterations are required before formerly a vaccine is introduced in the market. These trials are very costly and by making use of the generative AI, pharmaceutical companies and researchers can save a lot of money and resources. The technology uses offered them the opportunity to conduct these iterations in a cost-effective and efficient manner. The insightful information offered by the data science and AI can also help in trials and by correlating the results of different kinds of vaccine trials the technology can help further bring down the cost factor. The industry has already started using data science and AI in the vaccine discovery process, and while using the technology in different kinds of procedures, the overall time and cost related to drug discovery will definitely going to be shorter than before ([Floreano & Wood, 2015](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib6)). The machine learning aspect of data science and AI is very helpful in the vaccine discovery phase, and training the machines on identifying the ideal candidates for the trials of the vaccines and then correlating the results of how the trials have brought a positive change is very important and significant for the vaccine discovery process. The technology also very useful in managing the impact of the new vaccines, the data and analysing its impacts, the data science and AI can be widely used for preserving the data management related to the important vaccine discovery for the future too. The process related to cleaning the data and generation of the various queries related to the possible impact of the new vaccines on health can also be preserved with the help of generative AI. Especially when it comes to the decoding process related to various biomedical processes, the usability of the data science and AI is very crucial. In fact, the utility of data science and AI in discovering new drugs and vaccines is holistic and encompassing. From identifying the molecules related to the targeted diseases to finding the right candidate for administration of the vaccine under the trial and tracking the results with the purpose of preserving the information for the years to come, all things can be done very efficiently with the help of the generative AI. The primary benefit related to the use of technology in drug and vaccine Discovery is a reduction in the cost as many research reports have predicted that the then use of data science and AI can bring down the cost to one-tenth of the total amount required as an investment to come up with a new vaccine ([Fox, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib7)).

#### 2.3 Automating Administrative Processes

In comparison to the other sectors, the automation enabled by the data science and AI in healthcare is very critical for improving the overall delivery experience to the patients. Speed of processing the requirements to refer the patient to the doctor can become very critical and even decide the fate of the entire treatment procedure. It also makes sense as the focus of the healthcare management services must be on delivering superior patient care rather than focusing on completing files, managing bills and other associated aspects related to the healthcare sector. By achieving better efficiency in the administrative processes, data science and AI can play a crucial role in enhancing the overall diagnostic and treatment procedures for patients ([Ghosh, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib8)). The automated process can also be related to gathering the data and information of patients, and by combining it with the analysis of their present conditions, a complete record related to particular patient can be made for reference purposes. The sharing of the report among the participating stakeholders in the healthcare ecosystem will then lead to the creation of a dynamic and vibrant healthcare sector. The need or to meet processes and treatment procedures is also being felt to divert the critical human resources on other important parameters of the healthcare system ([Hurley & Adebayo, 2017](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib9)). The utilisation of the manpower in the hospital must be done in a judicious manner, and by automating healthcare features and systems with minimum human intervention, the enhancement in the efficiency of the system can be achieved in a significant manner. The automation of data collection, analyses and processing is another significant benefit of generative AI. Of specially, the ability of the technology to extrapolate the data for to predicting future business scenarios is quite commendable. The current crunch of manpower that the World Health Care ecosystem is experiencing today is very acute. According to the leading authority in the field of healthcare ecosystem, it is estimated that around 30 million people are required to fill management positions in hospitals, dispensaries and infirmaries in order to provide satisfactory healthcare services to the targeted people. This number is bound to increase in the coming times with the population expected to rise and reach the level of 9 billion by the end of 2050. In order to offer quality and affordable healthcare services to such a number of people across the globe, technology adoption is a prerequisite and primary criterion to focus upon by all players in the healthcare ecosystem ([Kopalle et al., 2020](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib10)). The use of data science and AI can also be done to issue different kinds of statements required by both regulatory bodies and customers along with filling out records of the patients in relation to their medical conditions. These are things in addition to the enormous utility of data science and AI in digitising the records that can be used for a whole lot of purposes by both industry players and regulatory organisations.

#### 2.4 Remote Operations

The use of data science and AI is increasingly finding favour among stakeholders, thanks to its capability to offer remote assistance to patients. This utility is specifically significant for elderly people who find it difficult to commute to the hospitals for their appointments with healthcare professionals. Also, people who are suffering from acute diseases which leave them almost in still positions can also utilise the technology to get remote assistance from the doctors and physicians. With the help of data science and AI and IoT, the dream of receiving quality healthcare services is fast becoming a reality ([Olsen, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib11)). This aspect of getting assistance remotely is also proving beneficial for countries in developing and underdeveloped economies. The small towns and villages in these countries do not have quality healthcare services, and by using advanced technology such as telemedicine applications, the patients get a chance to have treatment from the best doctors in the world. This also saves time for the practitioners by allowing them to offer treatment services to a large number of people without actually needing to visit them in person. By combining the generative aspect of AI with natural language processing (NLP) models, desirable progress can be achieved in delivering quality healthcare services to target patients. The primary advantage of these telemedicine applications is their round the clock availability, which is a big plus for patients ([Pal & Shalender, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib12)). The best thing about remote assistance is that not only physicians or doctors offer medical services but people associated with the medical fraternity are also able to offer these services to patients. Nurses, junior doctors and attendants can use the telemedicine services to offer assistance to patients worldwide. The concept of virtual nurses is fast gaining prominence across the world is also natural extension of the generative AI. The critical role of the data scientists in making the processes more efficient and effective is also recognised across the globe. The data science has proved instrumental in bringing the AI revolution in the healthcare sector, and by offering the collection, analysis and evaluation of the relevant data, the healthcare sector is becoming increasingly effective in treating patients cost-effectively and affordably. The significance here lies in integrating data science with data science and AI as the combination is the one that has the transformative capability to change the very face of the global healthcare sector ([Pelley, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib13)). The cost associated with travelling and bringing the patients in person for the treatment process can also be significantly decreased with the help of data science and AI technologies. Another important point to note is that all these revolutions are catalysed by the availability of high-speed internet, and by ensuring that the connectivity remains available across the globe, policymakers can ensure that the healthcare sector will continue to benefit from the combined capabilities of data science and AI.

### 3\. Development of Holistic Framework

#### 3.1 Develop Vision

In order to take the organisation through the adoption process of data science and AI, the healthcare ecosystem must have a collective vision of the direction they want to head into (refer [Fig. 1.1](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#fig1)). This vision is one of the primary requirements for making the technology adoption a successful process. It is also important to integrate the technology adoption with long-term profitability of the company so that each and every person recognises the importance of becoming a coriander in the constantly changing business environment. Organisations usually ignoring of specific technology visions but given the importance of new-age innovations in offering the sustainability and a competitive edge to organisations, this development is a must ([Shalender & Yadav, 2018](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib17), [2019](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib18)). The delegation of the top management in this development must have input from the business and functional level people; these are the persons who will be directly responsible for the implementation of the data science and AI strategy in the company. It would be more useful if the entire ecosystem of healthcare could develop a collective vision towards adoption of the technology, and by sharing the cost in terms of the resources required to do this digital transformation, the organisations in the healthcare sector can save a lot of money in the process. It is therefore necessary that healthcare companies must be attention to development of the technology vision and align that with the overall goals, objectives and values of the organisation.

#### 3.2 Start Small

The very first recommendation is to adopt these technologies in an incremental manner, and rather than changing the entire system and replacing it with the technology part, it is better to start using data science and AI in an implemental manner ([Shalender & Sharma, 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib15)). This will not only save the cost associated with the adoption of the technology but will also help the organisations to tackle resistance that might arise due to adoption of the technology in a big way. Especially when it comes to the legacy systems which are operational for a long period of time in healthcare organisations, replacing all those systems with data-driven decision-making might not augur well to the adoption process. Top management must take into confidence each and every employee about the adoption prospects of data science so that the resistance related to this adoption process can be minimised.

![image](images/f01-01.jpg)

Fig. 1.1. Practical Guidelines. *Source:* Authors' conceptualisation.

This approach will help in gaining the confidence of the shareholders who might also not like the process of a comprehensive change in a single instance ([Shalender, 2018](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib14)). As resources required for the transformation and adoption of the technology has to be approved by the shareholders of the organisation, the positive attitude of the shareholders is a prerequisite for determining the success of the process. It is also helpful if the top management conveys their idea about adopting data science and the return form and circulating that memorandum to all the employees so that they can have a clear idea about the digital strategy of the organisation ([Shalender et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib16)). This clarity will help in making sure that adoption of the data science and AI will be accomplished in a satisfactory manner.

#### 3.3 Train Employees

This is one of the most neglected issues and has to be addressed on a priority basis as far as the adoption of data science and AI in the healthcare sector is concerned. By investing in the training and development of employees, organisations can make them ready to use the technology efficiently ([Singla & Shalender, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib19)). This is also a credible method to reduce the opposition to the technology adoption as informed and trained employees are more likely to adopt the technology without any kind of inhibition than others. Of course investing in the training and development is a function of resources and once again edition if the healthcare sector decides to train employees on the new age innovations, it will cost them money. The approval for training and development of employees on the technology from usually comes from the top management of the organisation with corporate-level people in turn asking shareholders for the same in order to get the approval. The entire process can become more streamlined and smoother if the technology adoption in the company is associated with the long-term profitability of the organisation ([Singla et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib20)). It is the responsibility of technology team to demonstrate the benefits associated with the technology adoption, and by offering tangible benefits that can be showcased in front of top management and shareholders, the way forward to adopt and implement the technology can be smoothened. Investing in the training and development of the employees has always been a good idea as it inculcates a sense of belongingness and familiarity among employees. This will also help to reduce role stress and work disengagement, leading to better performance on critical parameters such as growth, market share, customer retention, etc.

#### 3.4 Correlate Results

One of the significant steps that companies often field to implement is to correlate the results of technology adoption with the market parameters. As an organisation, you must demonstrate that because of the implementation of the technology adoption, crucial parameters related to the performance have been infected positively. Correlating the data science and use of AI with enhanced patient satisfaction, delivery of superior value to patients and offering affordable healthcare services is a part of the process that you must complete to enhance the implementation of the technology in your organisation. By demonstrating superior results to the top management, healthcare companies can ensure that they get hold of more resources in order to take the use of technology in their companies to a higher level. Communicating the results with the positive framework is not only related to the technology adoption in the healthcare company at the present stage but also to take it to another level; more resources might be required to drive the next stage of digital transformation in the company. The communication of the result is important for the shareholders as they always remain concerned about their investment in video functional departments and operational mechanisms ([Wedel & Kannan, 2016](#978-1-83608-298-920251001.xhtml#bib21)). By demonstrating the positive value of technology adoption through the demonstration of superior performance, profitability and customer acquisition, healthcare organisations can ensure that they keep getting the resources for further integration of the technology in the business. The positive impact of the technology will likely to be converted into good results for the healthcare company in the financial markets too which will come as an added advantage for the sector.

### 4\. Conclusion and Discussion

There is no doubt about the game-changing potential of data science and AI. Both these technologies are proving instrumental in taking the efficiency of the healthcare sector to the next level. To ensure that these technologies continue to offer significant benefits for the healthcare sector, the holistic approach of considering the entire ecosystem while implementing that technology needs to be taken into concentration. Each and every stakeholder who is participating in the healthcare sector must get a vision of technology integration, and accordingly, they must process it in organisations so that maximum benefit can be derived from the adoption of data science and AI. The industry players need to take a central role in the adoption of the technology so that other participating stakeholders such as suppliers, pharmaceutical companies, supply chain operators and customers also get the encouragement to do the same. While the heavy lifting related to the technology adoption has to be done by the corporate sector, the critical role of the policymakers couldn't be overemphasised here. Regulators, administrators and policymakers need to come out with open and constructive policies so that technology adoption in the healthcare sector can be achieved at a desired pace. The role of the new entrepreneurial ventures especially in the technology domain is also very crucial as they can help the cause by making the new technology affordable and cost-effective in terms of the adoption process. The requirement of resources continues to be on the higher side and by making data science and especially AI cost-effective in terms of their adoption process, the overall adoption of the technology in the healthcare sector can be catalysed by a significant margin. This also points towards the important role of venture capitalists and financial institutions in funding the technology domain in the healthcare sector as it will lead to innovations that can be offered to patients in a cost-effective manner. Given the importance of the healthcare sector in the overall ecosystem of public welfare, the coming together of the corporate sector and the policymakers is a must. It is only when both these stakeholders join forces, that the entire ecosystem of the healthcare sector can be developed from the technology perspective. The revolution of technology in the healthcare industry has just begun, and it is a matter of time before we start technology taking over all the important aspects of healthcare parameters. In sum, the need of the hour is to adopt a holistic approach that is aimed to offer superior benefits to all stakeholders. This creation of a win–win situation is something that everyone in the ecosystem should strive for because ultimately the sustenance of the technology option in the healthcare sector depends upon whether all participating stakeholders get benefit out of it.

## Chapter 2

## The Next Wave in Marketing: Data Science in the Age of Generative AI

M. Kalaivani^a, Suganya V^a., N.V. Suresh^b and S. Catherine^c

^aSRM Institute of Science and Technology, India

^bASET College of Science and Technology, India

^cSaveetha Engineering College, India

### Abstract

In the current market trend, generative adversarial networks (GANs) and generative pre-trained transformers (GPTs) are highly revolutionising content marketing. The capacity of data analysts and marketers to predict the trends, clear campaigns and provide tailored customer experiences improves business development. The generative artificial intelligence (AI) is a significant challenge to the marketer since it created ethical concerns such as data privacy, ethical dilemma, bias in content creation and misleading information. Ensuring the data usage needs regulatory framework for the AI-generated data. Marketers and data analysts should be vigilant in adopting the best practices of balancing applications of AI. The study explores the intersection of marketing and AI by literature reviewing articles from the Scopus database from the year 2002 to 2023. The study aims to provide a comprehensive overview of AI-based analysis in current competitive research. It employs a systematic approach to examine existing literature using Scopus database and structured research questions. The focus is on assessing the research landscape, identifying key AI-based sentiment analysis approaches and understanding and opportunities. While sentiment research is currently a valuable tool, it’s value lies not in the data it collects but in how that data is used to drive meaningful action. The creation of proper regulatory terms and clear ethical practices for the potential AI integration in marketing. AI's potential lies in its ability to enhance our understanding of customers and guide us towards more effective strategies.

*Keywords*: Generative AI; ethical considerations; data privacy; regulatory frameworks; consumer behaviour

### 1\. Introduction

The application of artificial intelligence (AI) in marketing dates back to the 1950s, when advances in computational theory laid the foundation for simple rule-based systems and machine learning (ML) algorithms. Expert systems and executive information systems became available after the 1980s, enabling marketers to use AI for customer profiling, recommendations and business operations decisions. Big data in the 1990s signalled a change in the field towards data mining and predictive analytics, allowing marketers to glean insights for specific audiences from sizeable datasets. Automation and ML gained popularity in the 2000s, revolutionising processes like content marketing personalisation and email marketing. The 2010s saw substantial advancements in natural language processing (NLP) and the widespread use of chatbots, which revolutionised conversational marketing. Today, generative AI technologies enable marketers to create hyper-personalised content and deliver tailored experiences that resonate with individual consumers, highlighting the ongoing evolution and transformative impact of AI in marketing.

AI is creating new era and revolutionising the marketing new landscape, offering innovative tools and techniques to enhance customer engagement, personalise experiences and optimise marketing strategies. By leveraging AI, businesses can gain deeper insights into consumer behaviour, predict market trends and deliver more targeted and effective marketing campaigns.

Generative AI refers to ML algorithms that enable computers to create new, original content from pre-existing data. This technology has the potential to revolutionise various industries by improving customer satisfaction, efficiency, personalisation and creativity. Generative AI models are trained on learnt patterns and examples to produce unique content, such as text, images and music. These algorithms generate realistic output without the need for direct human intervention.

Generative AI is a game-changer in marketing, revolutionising how brands connect with their audience and create content. Its power lies in generating highly personalised, relevant and engaging content at scale, leveraging data and algorithms to drive marketing strategies forward.

The way that brands interact with their audience and produce content is being completely transformed by generative AI, which is a marketing game changer. Its capacity to produce highly customised, pertinent and captivating content at scale – using data and algorithms to propel marketing strategies – is what gives it its power.

### 2\. Objective of the Study

*   To understand the data science influence in the new generative AI,
*   To study the current trend in AI data science in marketing and
*   To describe the AI applications in marketing.

The focus is on assessing the research landscape, identifying key AI-based analysis approaches and understanding the challenges and opportunities associated with this technique in competitive research.

### 3\. Tools in AI Influencing Factors in Marketing

![image](images/f02-01.jpg)

Fig. 2.1. New Age of Marketing AI Tools. *Source:* Author framework about the current AI data science tools in marketing.

#### 3.1 Personalised Marketing

It is highly prioritised in marketing campaigns by doing consumer analysis. The behaviour pattern also can be understandable through personalised product recommendation, email messages, product recommendation and content creating for targeted advertisements.

These data serve as the foundation for creating personalised information, recommendations and marketing campaigns tailored to each individual client. AI enables advertisements that cater to each customer's unique needs and preferences ([Lee et al., 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib15)). Traditional personalised marketing strategies typically involve segmentation and rule-based techniques. In contrast, AI methods use advanced ML algorithms to assess client information and make predictions. The advantages of AI in personalised marketing are evident in its enhanced efficiency and accuracy. However, it also raises concerns, particularly regarding privacy and high costs.

##### 3.1.1 AI-Powered Chatbots

Nowadays in today's market activities, AI-powered chatbots and virtual assistants are being used for customer service, lead generation with the customer and sales support. These AI cited services will provide instant result in your marketing activities. This will improve the customer experience.

Human–machine interaction is strongly related to the use of chatbots in marketing. [Kaczorowska (2019)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib11) asserts that chatbots are useful in social media activities because they offer pre-defined information for comprehending and evaluating reality. Chatbots are employed in marketing techniques. A well-thought-out chatbot may help customers save time and effort by assisting them with different transaction phases. Chatbot also act as a differentiator for all businesses, making it stand out from the competitors. Chatbots are more friendly user, more real and personable than programmes that are already on the market, especially when it comes to the way they make contact, have discussions and try to establish a rapport.

Chatbots are playing prominent role in customer service, and most of the customer services are done through the chatbots because it's providing quick support to the customer in many digital platforms by solving the queries. NLP and ML are used by AI-driven chatbots to comprehend consumer inquiries, provide pertinent information and enable easy transactions. Chatbots is one of the effective tools for increasing customer happiness and engagement because of their continual availability and quick answers. Furthermore, by using user preferences and previous interactions as a basis, chatbots may provide tailored recommendations and solutions for the better experience to the customers. Chatbots provide better platform to the customer adapt to better anticipate and satisfy client demands through ongoing learning and development, which helps to provide a more efficient and productive customer experience.

##### 3.1.2 AI Predictive Analytics

Predictive analytics used to understand the market predictions. In the current AI generative market, the consumer behaviour has been analysed with a large data set, and it is very easy to predict the future of marketing. This helps in making data-based decisions and optimising the suitable marketing strategies for the business.

There was a study conducted on the assumption that management awareness mediates the relationship between AI predictive analytics and marketing strategies, that is the management of awareness is playing a major role to predict the marketing strategies, and this was proven through analysis. It is found that management awareness was able to provide a crucial link between AI and marketing strategies. Marketing efforts normally involve huge amount of data in which AI can classify and improve the data according to the requirements ([Alshaketheep et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib1)). Therefore, the role of AI in marketing is likely to be hybrid where AI applications and analysis provide insights and information related to marketing strategies, but the managers have to modify the data according to their need.

##### 3.1.3 Voice Search Optimization

It is one of the tools which all marketers are using for attracting the customers to have a good experience about the product. With the rise of voice assistants like Siri and Alexa, marketers are optimising their content for voice search queries. This involves using natural language keywords and creating voice-friendly content.

The use of technology marketing tools implies the development of data transfer protocols that ensure that integrating many digital technologies will have a synergistic effect. ‘APIs’, or application programme interfaces, are made to function with different software interfaces. Consequently, a website and a DMP platform communicate with CRM. Next, when a customer fills out a purchase form on a website, he also provides CRM data, and a DMP platform determines the customer's source, the cost of bringing him to the website, what he does there and how to best leverage connections with him.

Virtual reality (VR) and augmented reality (AR) are both virtual and enhanced. With the use of special glasses, one may experience the artificially constructed reality known as VR. Through the use of specialised software, AR emphasis the inclusion of things in the actual world by showcasing them in a virtual setting and offering the ability to interact with them in specific ways. As a result, they employ virtual representations of flats that a prospective buyer might digitally decorate and renovate while marketing real estate. The technology of AR allows transactions to be completed while minimising risks, saving time, money and human energy and enhancing the emotional appeal of purchases. It achieves this without requiring detachment from the actual world and the automated voice agents.

##### 3.1.4 Content Generation

The advertisements for the product are generated very easily by using AI and AI tools, and also these tools are being used to generate content to develop products, product description, and for all social media posts. It's also providing high quality content for the advertisements.

AI has been used for SEO to analyse the data and to optimise the data to have better visibility for the website.

AI automation tools can analyse search engine algorithms and trends to optimise websites for better search engine rankings. This includes keyword optimization, content recommendations, and backlink analysis.

AI has the competence to do the personalised brand experiences for the customer, thereby its developing user engagement and customer loyalty. Marketers utilise language-based AI as engagement managers, payment processors and sales tools to enhance the user experience. Customer are not necessary to purchase independently, instead they can rely on AI chatbots to purchase. AI serves better language-based learning from their earlier mistakes in purchase and it's making the customer to very clear to earn better results over a time, It helps the marketer to understand the customers interest. Through observation, data collection, and analysis, AI facilitates content personalisation. This technology is particularly beneficial for email campaigns, helping marketers optimise their efforts. Email marketing is a digital marketing service that ensures effective conversion strategies and reaches the target audience at the right time (Haleem et al., 2022). A significant advantage of AI in marketing is its data analysis capability, allowing marketers to derive actionable insights from vast amounts of data.

##### 3.1.5 AI Automatic Tools

It serves the marketer to reduce their time in framing mails, crating content for social media schedules. This motives the marketer to focus the better marketing strategy to market the product in this competitive world very easily. Another important tool concern in AI for the marketer is data privacy and less understanding of AI algorithms. There is enough awareness among the marketer and focus on ethical AI practices gives transparency, fairness and making every marketer to be responsible for customer data.

Marketing automation encompasses several aspects of marketing decision-making. Further AI automation “offers improved productivity superior decision-making, elevated returns on marketing investments, and heightened customer satisfaction and loyalty” ([Heimbach et al., 2015](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib9)); the automated customisation of marketing decision support is the essential element of marketing automation. These days, the business environment automatically generates enormous amounts of data ([Bucklin et al., 2002](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib5); Hoffman et al., 2022), which allows marketers to use marketing automation software to identify preferences, create effective proposals, and respond adaptively to customer, competitor, and influencer behaviour. Based on criteria established by certain software and algorithms, this abundance of data makes it easier to make automatic decisions for marketing initiatives. Using social media or search engine analytics to activate targeted advertising is one example of how these algorithms use data input analysis to develop predictions and behaviours, sometimes nearly instantly.

Marketers that have used computerised marketing systems are forced to reinterpret the application of heuristic principles while also expanding their reach. Technological change is seen as significant by marketers, but in contrast to the new environment that autonomous marketing systems arise in, the ability to learn and adapt is even more apparent. When it comes to providing inputs to the new systems, regulating their operation, and assessing their outcomes, the utilisation of these new systems alters both the volume and, more importantly, the quality of decision-making duties. Put another way, automation appears to alter the field of application (scope) of heuristic decision-making models rather than substitute them.

##### 3.1.6 Sentiment Analysis

Effective strategic competitive research necessitates knowing consumer perceptions of competitors and the factors influencing them. This will help ascertain the actual merits and shortcomings of the different company components. Information gathered for business purposes through market research is included in this. One of the best ways to accomplish this is with text analytics and sentiment analysis. In order to produce a report based on consumer perceptions, sentiment analysis contrasts a brand's products and services with those of its competitors. These insightful figures can assist brands in differentiating themselves from rivals. Businesses are beginning to see sentiment analysis driven by AI as a critical tool ([Taherdoost & Mitra, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib28)). It makes it easier to understand what customers are thinking. It is also helpful for understanding the thoughts and emotions of employees. It is typically used to understand customer reviews, social media references, the seriousness of a service issue, etc. One could argue that sentiment analysis will be used in the near future to explore people's emotions in greater detail. It will enhance computers' ability to understand the feelings expressed in comments, messages, and feedback. Companies will be able to react more specifically as a result. The kind of study was to review the literature on AI-based sentiment analysis in order to provide future researchers with a better understanding of the field's advancements and to help them stay current with current developments.

Sentiment analysis enables marketers to comprehend quantitative methodologies with profound and reliable insights into the attitudes and opinions of service or product consumers. One application area is marketing, which makes it possible to identify preferences, attitudes, and opinions on social media networks about products, services, companies, and travel destinations. In word clouds, high frequency words are arranged more centrally and are shown in a font size that corresponds to how often the word appears in the text. This is where the analysis's distinctive value lies. This study provides all the essential R programming language features and sentiment analysis procedures needed to perform sentiment analysis with Mostar as the travel destination. It is an extremely useful tool for marketing analysis because of its ability to convert large amounts of textual data in real-time.

### 4\. AI Applications in Marketing

AI tools in marketing applications the researcher Introduces GPT-3, a most powerful generative AI model most capable of producing human-like text [Radford et al. (2019)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib21). The paper also discussed about the applications in marketing including the personalised communication and content generation for social media posts etc.

AI is more effective than humans at identifying patterns in marketing and can respond to new information faster, AI is gradually replacing humans and service accurate solutions in the business decision in this capacity (Gkikas & Theodoridis, 2019). AI can predict with accuracy how and when your ideal consumers will make purchases if given the correct data. Algorithms in AI may be used in marketing automation in the wide range of activities (Reimers et al., 2016).

One method AI digital marketing helps companies and marketers save time and money is through automated digital marketing services (Nair & Gupta, 2021). AI helps create high-performing content and makes decision-making easier. Businesses that employ AI-powered digital marketing techniques may be able to more successfully target the right clients with enticing offers ([Van Esch & Black, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib30)).

### 5\. Digital Marketing and AI

AI has integrated in digital marketing and improve the effectiveness of the advertisements [Aslam (2022)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib3) in the real market. Many of the digital marketers are believing today the AI tools for the content creation in social media post.

Popular social networks such as Facebook, LinkedIn, Instagram, and others employ ML models to recommend users or accounts to follow, jobs to apply for, identify images, monitor ongoing conversations, and more ([Kaplan & Haenlein, 2020](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib12)). According to the Gartner data from 2019, 37% of businesses utilise AI for content management and curation. ML and NLP models may get social insights by analysing a brand's historical performance, which can then be utilised to provide consumer recommendations ([Anandvardhan, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib2)).

The daily social networks we use, such as Facebook and Twitter, rely heavily on AI. Facebook recognises faces in tagged photos, displays advertisements, sends pop-ups, and shows posts that are similar to those with which a user has already interacted ([Anandvardhan, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib2)). Facebook also employs powerful ML applications of AI for these purposes. Facebook owns Instagram, a social media platform that use AI to identify and remove fraudulent content. Snapchat tracks users' faces and adds effects that move in real-time with their faces using computer vision, an AI technology.

LinkedIn uses AI to assist advertisers target audiences, measure conversions, display specialised information in feeds, recommend jobs, advise users they might want to connect with, and automate bidding ([Kaplan & Haenlein, 2020](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib12); [Kessinger, 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib13)).

### 6\. AI Tool Applications in Marketing

AI tools applications in marketing activities has emerged and irreplaceable ([Jarek & Mazurek, 2019](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib10)) the authors discussed in this paper about the personalisation to the customer to improve the customer experience and customer engagement, this will leads to customer conversion rates. The applications of AI have been widely explored across various business sectors. It is expected that AI will profoundly influence business operations and everyday life. Despite being in its early stages [Khoa (2021)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib14), AI is already integrated into daily activities through website chatbots, smartphone virtual assistants, and NLP for interpreting voice commands.

### 7\. AI-Powered Tools in Customer Engagement

It is found that the AI-powered tools are playing major role in marketing and we are in AI driven market [Tuzhilin (2020)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib29). The paper highlights the future trend in marketing and the potential benefit to the marketer and the consumer explained. AI tools helping them to frame marketing strategies.

According to the different brand factors, AI used to measure brand evaluation [Nufer and Muth (2022)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib19). AI can also be used to reinforce the customer journey by tracking the entire lifecycle of the customer journey, from raising brand awareness to fostering loyalty and brand equity ([Rana et al., 2022)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib22). Businesses may increase awareness, CRM, and overall customer loyalty through using AI-based solutions like chatbots, recommenders, AI-powered assistants, and interactive voice recognition (IVR). Recently, AI has significantly impacted marketing by enhancing the customer experience across various stages. Notably, medium- and small-sized enterprises now have access to resources that were previously available only to large corporations (Kupec & Pisar, 2021; Oosthuizen et al., 2021). Neural networks are providing marketers with dynamic tools to better understand consumer behaviour, develop sophisticated buyer segments, automate marketing tasks, create content, and forecast sales. These tools enable the processing of large data sets to generate more significant insights. By leveraging patterns from previous campaigns, marketers can utilise predictive analytics to anticipate campaign outcomes. Although neural networks have been around for some time, the increasing need to process Big Data has made these systems much more dynamic and intelligent (Hassan & De Filippi, 2021; Mgiba & Nyamande, 2020).

### 8\. AI Automation

In [2018, Davenport and Ronanki](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib7). This article discusses the real-world uses of AI in a business of sectors, including marketing it explained customer engagement through personalisation. It also emphasises how operational marketing activities can be automated by AI tools, freeing up the marketers to concentrate and creative marketing strategies.

[Taddy (2018)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib27) looks into potential advancements in AI technology and how the marketing field might be influenced. He draws close attention to the moral conundrums raised by the application of AI in marketing, concentrating in particular on issues with bias and data privacy. Taddy emphasises how critical it is for marketers to confront these problems head-on in order to guarantee the ethical and responsible application of AI technologies.

A thorough overview on digital marketing strategies is presented by [Chaffey and Ellis-Chadwick (2019)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib6), who emphasise the significance of AI and advanced analytics of data. Their research offers helpful insight on how to use these technologies to meet organisational goals and improve marketing efficacy. [Van Esch and Black (2021)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib30) this paper explains that how AI improves the digital marketing and explores the prominent advancements and their practical implications in the future. It also discusses potential transformation about AI and various activities in digital marketing. [Brei (2020)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib4). From this article, it is explored how AI and ML are influencing the workflow in marketing. In this current scenario, AI and ML cannot be separated in the normal day today operational activities. [Ljepava (2022)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib16) study describes the comprehensive usage of AI tools in marketing and how it is helpful in taking decision in marketing. It also found that AI implications in marketing process and preparation of strategy for business. [Waller and Fawcett (2013)](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib31) explore the AI tools application in supply chain management and the advancement technologies of predictive analysis and big data and discusses the impact of predictive analytics and big data on supply chain management, parallelling these advancements with their implications for marketing and strategic decision-making.

### 9\. Ethical Issues in Applications of AI in Marketing

Benkert (2019) ethical issues of AI in marketing has concluded that there are eight prominent ethical issues of the applications of AI in marketing such as data privacy, customer data bias accountability of the marketer using AI, transparency in data and the marketers are inability to understand the ethical values and the impacts on social influence and it affects in many ways of social activities. The use of AI in marketing raises concerns about its influence on consumer behaviour. Many scholarly studies have explored predictive marketing systems and their ability to affect consumer choices, albeit in different contexts (de Marcellis-Warin et al., 2022). Currently, organisations frequently put action ahead of requesting consent and will later, if needed, submit a statement of regret. This strategy is unacceptable and unsustainable. There are large amounts of private information are being collected about people without their knowledge or consent; even fewer are aware of possible uses for this data. These ethical concerns need to be given the greatest attention by the government and researchers who have dedicated themselves to doing no harm and genuinely serving the public interest.

### 10\. Data Science and AI

Data science is laying the biggest role in AI. The capacity of AI to handle a broad range of data formats is another data format required. In addition to interpreting numerical data, AI can also comprehend text, images, and voice sound inputs, delivering context and meaning for subsequent evaluation of the data (Dhar, 2016). Unsupervised learning remains highly challenging. This field is expected to see increased activity, particularly regarding unstructured data, which vastly outnumbers structured data and continues to grow exponentially. Although much of this data consists of chatter, there is also valuable human content that could be utilised by machines in the future, once designers or the machines themselves learn to interpret it. However, machines currently struggle with many simple unsupervised learning tasks that humans easily complete or tasks requiring common sense. Achieving AI that matches human capabilities is still a distant goal.

### 11\. Impact of AI in Marketing

In marketing today, we are working on new generative AI it came in to existence last year. The biggest question now is the know how technology about the generative AI and many of the people in marketing are working hard to learn, and it has certainly been fundamentally changing the marketing world for some time. In marketing, AI tools help the marketer to understand customer segmentation, in finding new customers who show propensity to buy, in product recommendations, in sales, in customer support activities, in the production of advertising creating content messages, in the generation of responses on social networks, in doing research on behaviour, in algorithms for predicting buyer behaviour, etc.

### 12\. AI Tools for the Marketing Field

#### 12.1 Hyper Personalisation

By analysing the huge data with the help of AI we can find out the customer preference and behaviours by using sentiment analysis which increases the customer engagement, conversion rate of the customer and the loyalty of the customer.

#### 12.2 Customer Insights and Analytics

AI tools are exploring large datasets can be processed and interpreted much more quickly by AI-powered analytics tools than by conventional techniques. AI provide information about consumer behaviour, consumer experience, industry future trends, and the effectiveness of Advertisement campaigns, assisting marketers in making data-driven choices.

#### 12.3 Predictive Analytics

AI is able to predict and comprehend future trends in customer and consumer behaviour based on historical data. This makes it possible for marketers to more effectively target campaigns, optimise inventory, and react proactively to customer requests – all of which improve outcomes and save expenses.

#### 12.4 Automated Content Creation

AI-driven tools can generate content, such as product descriptions, social media posts, and even articles, quickly and at scale. This helps maintain a consistent content output and frees up human resources for more strategic tasks.

#### 12.5 Chatbots and Customer Service

Chatbots and virtual assistants driven by AI offer immediate customer help by managing routine questions and tasks. By giving prompt answers, this increases customer satisfaction and frees up human agents to address more complicated problems.

#### 12.6 Ad Targeting and Programmatic Advertising

By evaluating user data, AI improves ad targeting by displaying more relevant advertisements to the appropriate audience. AI-powered programmatic advertising optimises ad expenditure and boosts return on investment by automating real-time ad buying.

#### 12.7 SEO and Content Optimization

Optimisation of the business website is very important for all the business, AI technologies analyse trends, keywords, and competition strategies to assist improve website content for search engines. Better search engine rankings and more organic visitors result from this ([Fig. 2.1](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#fig1)).

### 13\. Conclusion

This paper studied the growing research areas of generative AI in marketing. By scrutinising the advent of generative AI marks, a transformative era in the field of data science. The innovative ideas add to a new level of creativity and problem solving in marketing decisions. It improves the analytical skills of the marketer to understand the consumer behaviour. The development of AI data science and the predictive analysis will be possible to create effective generative AI models for marketing activities. The main problem of using generative AI and the speed of applications with lack of data. Generative AI is providing the new business model, opportunities and possibilities to solve many problems in the marketing. It is anticipating the new products in the market, new strategy to the marketer, and new services to the customer develop the business in the real world ([Fornazaric, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251002.xhtml#bib8)).
