Alberth (2023) describes “Use of AI” as specific applications of generative AI as shown in [Table 7.1](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#tbl1) used by academics for assignments, term papers, research papers and other academic responsibilities. Before using a technology, users should assess its benefits or efficacy based on the study results ([Pechenkina, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib56)). According to [Dencik et al. (2023)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib18), customers are more likely to adopt and use the technology when they find it beneficial and able to increase their productivity and ease of operation. [Gallent-Torres et al. (2023)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib24) suggests that academics are more likely to accept the new technology if they perceive it as a means of enhancing the efficiency or quality of their work. The scholars' motivation to utilize Artificial Intelligence in Education (AIED) will be influenced by their evaluations of its advantages for their academic pursuits ([Khan, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib36)). AI technologies offer a wide range of features that greatly aid academic writers, addressing different stages of research and publication. Contemporary equipment is facilitating several alterations in the field of academic research. Examining sentences and contemplating various writing philosophies lead to the use of precise and refined language. In order to comprehend a sentence thoroughly, it is necessary to examine its syntactic, semantic, and structural elements ([Mahapatra, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib46)). Large-scale text processing refers to the efficient administration of enormous amounts of textual material. It has the ability to handle large volumes of data and execute complex computations, which allows for the generation of valuable insights ([Pigg, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib58)). Predictive modeling utilizes the input to anticipate the creation of words and generate coherent and contextually appropriate language. Further generative AI combines predictions with studied facts to create compelling writing that adheres to grammar rules, maintains coherence, and aligns with the intended meaning such as

*Writing and Editing Research Papers*: AI-driven writing tools facilitate organizing ideas into coherent and rational arguments.AI developed grammar, tone and style ensures maximum accuracy.

*Research Organization and Summarization*: AI technologies can efficiently search through large-scale databases to manage your literature review. By synthesizing important findings from multiple studies, it provides a comprehensive review without the need for detailed testing of each individual source.

*Research process recommendations*: By analyzing samples from a collection of published literature, generative AI can offer research techniques that can be extremely beneficial to your project. Generative AI systems specialize in extensive analysis and interpretation of data; hence, handling of large datasets has become more convenient.

*PDF Document Management*: There are a significant number of PDF files that are commonly viewed by academics. AI helps to identify, annotate, and categorize papers streamlining research administration.

Researchers and authors can greatly benefit from several generative AI applications, including

*   Automating repetitive tasks enhances time efficiency.
*   Enhanced precision during data analysis.
*   Enhanced writing proficiency accompanied by timely feedback.
*   Streamlined internal corporate procedures.
*   Acquiring knowledge through comprehensive analysis of data.

By integrating these generative AI tools into workflow, researchers may focus their attention on crucial areas of their work, such as formulating hypotheses, coordinating experiments, and constructing engaging narratives around their findings.

### 3\. Generative Artificial Intelligence Challenges

The challenges associated with technology use refer to the constraints, hurdles, or bottlenecks that prevent persons from utilizing technology ([Nguyen et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib53)). The term “AI issues” in this study refers to the different obstacles that prevent students from utilizing AI technologies. Technology adoption has been recognized as a significant challenge, especially for emerging or developing countries ([Kong et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib38)). Technological infringement occurs when potential customers see a given technology as challenging to use due to concerns related to availability, support, skill requirements, accessibility, and other similar factors. According to [Rojas (2024)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib63), individuals who find technology challenging tends to use it less or avoid using it altogether. Scholars will refrain from utilizing AI in the educational context if they perceive it as a formidable task. Individuals will employ AI for educational purposes if they are more inclined to do so.

### 4\. Views of Generative Artificial Intelligence in Academic Research

The general public is fond of generative AI models for their ability to provide information that is closer to human understanding. OpenAI introduced ChatGPT and DALL-E in 2022, which are widely acclaimed. GPT-based variations were widely recognized and well-liked according to [Lim et al. (2023)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib43). According to [Stephens et al. (2023)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib74), ChatGPT has exceptional expertise in writing structured articles and content, developing chatbot responses, facilitating language translation, resolving inquiries, and offering coding assistance. Researchers have demonstrated a keen interest in improving large language models (LLMs) for particular tasks and investigating the possibilities of cross-domain transfer learning ([Zuber & Gogoll, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib85)). ChatGPT facilitates the achievement of several educational objectives for both teachers and students. ChatGPT facilitates students in acquiring knowledge in a variety of subjects, enhances their educational attainment, addresses complex challenges, and promptly addresses educational inquiries. ChatGPT's programming activities enhance students' skills. [Alberth (2023)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib3) and [Stahl and Eke (2024)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib73) describe the cognitive benefits of ChatGPT. The year 2023 will be associated with Albreth while 2024 will be linked with Stahl and Eke. Benefits include enhancing the inherent motivation for learners to engage in research, enhancing their understanding of the subject matter, and cultivating them. Proficiency and automation are essential to enhance instructor/reviewer productivity and facilitate the development of research. In addition, publishers are adopting more effective diagnostic techniques to enhance evaluation/review system.

![image](images/f07-03.jpg)

Fig. 7.3. Views of Generative Artificial Intelligence in Academic Research. *Source:* Authors' own conceptualization.

This change in the evaluation method gives greater importance to the development of knowledge through indicators and conclusions drawn. This illustrates the relationship between critical and generative AI thinking as shown in [Fig. 7.3](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#fig3).

### 5\. Concerns About ChatGPT Use in Academic Research

Despite the numerous advantages it offers, the use of ChatGPT in an educational environment is accompanied by some glitches and disadvantages. There is growing concern that research and education on AI systems will contribute to the establishment and promotion of bias and discrimination against specific groups. In addition to current damage, changes or effects can lead to biased or inaccurate estimation of AI systems ([Alexander et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib4)). [Kong et al. (2024)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib38) explore the potential applications of ChatGPT in various domains such as education and to examine the issues raised by experts during the first three months of its implementation. Based on the results, ChatGPT exhibited different levels of performance on different topics. ChatGPT is dedicated to providing guidance, answering questions, assisting in the creation of instructional materials, conducting online tutorials, and supporting researchers through motivation. However, there were limitations to its implementation, such as the ability to falsify or manipulate data, as well as the ability to avoid detection by plagiarism detection programs.

### 6\. Ethical Issues in Generative Artificial Intelligence

[Kumar et al. (2024)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib41) argues that ChatGPT can improve learning outcomes because it helps with critical thinking, is good at providing tailored assistance, and is effective at structuring information. When used appropriately, generative AI like ChatGPT can help pupils with writing, research, and problem-solving tasks that are central to many different kinds of classes. And there are people who believe that being able to depend on a generative AI in this trustworthy way can also encourage students to express more individuality when they write because they're no longer constrained by what's considered “good” grammar or by the need to come up with entirely original ideas all the time ([Peng & Zhao, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib57)). It is vitally important to look into alleged cases of bias, intellectual theft, lack of authenticity, and cyber security threats. We must also confront the individuals who manipulate data for personal gain and the dissemination of false information in both professional and academic contexts ([Baldassarre & Padovan, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib9)). Using ChatGPT as an example, [Li et al. (2024)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib42) makes the argument that generative AI could raise fundamental ethical issues in education if used improperly. For instance, using AI to write essays could lead to what Li calls “the instantiation of a classic cheating engine.” Though ChatGPT's developers say their system can't currently write persuasive texts (i.e. claim something, then provide further justification), they also can't say with certainty that it won't be able to do so in the future.

### 7\. Brief Overview of Ethical Considerations of Generative AI

The link connecting individuality and authenticity has existed throughout human history. Since the dawn of AI-generated content, it has become much more challenging to distinguish between genuine creations and those generated by a computer. AI's ability to mimic real human intelligence threatens many traditional indicators of credibility because it can now produce very convincing lies ([Johri et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib32)). This development calls into question the reliability of all kinds of creative outputs, with serious repercussions for labor (especially political and journalistic labor) that relies on trust as a core component of its credibility ([De Gagne et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib17)). It's more difficult to check the work produced by generative AI. When these algorithms are trained using massive datasets, they can sometimes sound like they're writing entirely original text when in reality; they're just good at parroting what's been written before ([McMillan, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib49)). And because these systems are getting better all the time, the demand is increasing for new ways to spot when texts have been generated rather than written by humans. As AI systems become more widespread, concerns over fairness and bias in ethical considerations for generative AI are growing as well. Biased algorithms can exacerbate societal biases and lead to unjust outcomes. They have the ability to create deceit and misconceptions. Generative AI bias arises from the model's specific design and type of data used ([Rosário, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib64)). The poorly designed model can manipulate data and produce false or biased results even in the absence of any intentional bias ([Gouveia et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib26)). Ensuring equal treatment guarantees equal performance of AI systems for all user groups. The decision-making process is illustrated through the use of clarity and openness, especially in situations where generative AI has the potential to significantly impact the physical world ([Caliskan, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib15); [McCormack, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib48)). As the use of AI models extends to different scenarios, it becomes necessary for creative AI to have transparency and comprehension. Due to the ability of generative AI to produce several complex and diverse results, it is important for users to understand the underlying concepts and rationalities behind each resulting result. Transparency refers to the ability of the public to access and understand the creation, training set, and operation of the model of generative AI. A plausible model can justify its conclusions through logical reasoning.

If a generative AI generates information that is inaccurate or poses a threat, steps are taken to ensure those responsible are held accountable and take responsibility for their actions ([Spennemann et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib71)). The potential influence of AI output is the driving force behind the need to create accurate rules and procedures. Accepting responsibility for a portion of the results of an innovative AI system is tantamount to recognizing accountability ([Raman et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib60)). It is crucial to determine if a generative AI system is impaired or harms humans to hold that person accountable. Duty and accountability are related to moral and legal implications. The need to guarantee the proper functioning of AI is directly linked to accountability ([Zhu et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib83)). This role includes the design, execution, monitoring and establishment of AI systems.

Intellectual property addresses the rights of producers during the process of creating content, as well as the ownership of material generated by AI ([Johri et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib32)). There are concerns that the fundamental worth and pleasure derived from human production may be compromised if machines produce art, music, or literature. The financial beneficiaries of the development of economically efficient AI should be clearly discernible. The potential for inadvertent plagiarism, which involves reproducing copyrighted materials that have already been published, gives rise to ethical considerations regarding unintentional violations of intellectual property rights.

#### 7.1 The Economic Impact of Generative AI Is Twofold: Job Creation and Displacement

While generative AI has the ability to create new jobs, particularly in the technology sector, it also poses a risk of replacing existing occupations, particularly those that involve repetitive tasks or information generation ([Ray, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib61)). Generative AI has the potential to enhance tasks such as data processing, design, and content production, leading to improved outcomes and increased creativity ([Sidaoui et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib68)). Two examples of how these products and services can result in whole new business models are as follows: personalized content platforms and AI-powered design services. The societal impact could potentially influence education by diminishing the significance of interpersonal communication within the classroom and empowering AI tutors or content creators to tailor instruction ([Kumar et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib41)). The utilization of generative AI for the creation of deep fakes and fabricated news articles presents significant obstacles to the dissemination of information and the establishment of trust (Voorneveld, 2024).

Security and privacy are measures used to protect individuals, particularly when content generated by AI has the potential to distort or exploit them ([Li et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib42)). The importance of AI ethics is emphasized by the increasing number of applications for these models. To ensure that generative AI upholds human rights and provides users with a secure experience, these concerns must be addressed. Security issues encompass the manipulation, dependency, and tampering of data, whereas privacy concerns revolve around the leaking, monitoring, profiling, and customization of data ([Zlateva et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib84)). Data cannot be employed without explicit authorization, particularly in the context of training generative AI models.

The erosion of human abilities is associated with a decrease in opportunities for practice due to the ability of generative AI to autonomously develop content, design, or other outputs that typically require human labor ([Elbanna & Armstrong, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib22)). Inadequate opportunities to engage in regular practice can lead to a decline in skills over time. An excessive reliance on automation poses a risk when one becomes excessively dependent on generative AI. The potential dominance of generative AI in decision-making may overshadow the decline of human intuition or accumulated knowledge, both of which have historically played a vital role in several professions ([Andrieux et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib5)). The advent of generative AI has the potential to supplant human emotions, personal experience, and the generative AI domain, hence endangering the essence of creation ([Obrenovic et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib54)). The decisions produced by generative AI have the potential to lead to a decline in experiential learning and a potential devaluation of traditional abilities, as certain manual or traditional skills may become outdated ([Budhwar et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib13)). Undervaluation of certain skills notwithstanding their historical or cultural importance is also being observed.

### 8\. Generative Artificial Intelligence and Research

Undoubtedly, generative AI will persistently revolutionize research and its methodologies. The present study will be subject to a variety of consequences, some of which will have positive outcomes while others will raise concerns ([Mahapatra, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib46)). To illustrate the concerning impact of generative AI on research, particularly in the realm of academic journal publishing, the study emphasizes the rise in the number of low-quality and/or irrelevant paper submissions ([Li et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib42)). Before the development of ChatGPT, journal editors had to spend a significant amount of time assessing contributions that were either insufficient or unrelated. Typically, these submissions are presented to journals in one of two formats. Some manuscripts are clearly weak from the beginning. Among other things, this could involve research that is logically incorrect, argumentation that is weak, presentation of ideas that is ambiguous, or a lack of fresh ideas ([Alberth, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib3)). It is evident that writers are aiming for a superficial achievement to enhance their résumé when they neglect to thoroughly elaborate on their content. The second category, which is much more prevalent, consists of manuscripts that appear to be outside the scope of the journal ([Mahapatra, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib46)). Prior to submitting their work, it seems that the writers did not review the specific kind of research that the journal publishes. The majority of journal editors in the field will cite this as their main justification when questioned about why they decline submissions at the desk. It has been observed that about large number of manuscripts are being rejected without further consideration ([Lopezosa et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib45); [Nguyen et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib53)). The increasing influx of substandard and inconsequential manuscript submissions received by journals is a consequence of the mounting pressure on academics to publish in prestigious and esteemed periodicals. The extent of low-quality and inconsequential research, which is just used as a basis for publication and lacks scientific or societal significance, is perhaps even more concerning ([Bakla, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib8); [Caliskan, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib15); [Misra et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib50); [Montezuma & Chong, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib52)). The use of generative AI will expedite the spread of insignificant and subpar research, as well as the submission of irrelevant and defective papers, since individuals can now employ computer tools to generate significant portions of their publications. With the advent of ChatGPT and other comparable AI systems, it is reasonable to expect a surge in the number of articles submitted to academic publications. The growing influx of substandard and/or unrelated papers being submitted to the journal will exacerbate the workload of editors, associate editors, and reviewers, who frequently receive inadequate or no compensation from publishers or their respective universities for their editorial or reviewing duties. An advantage of generative AI in research and publications is that it promotes greater fairness in scholarly writing. However, AI is not responsible for its output and cannot function as an author. Its role is limited to aiding writers in articulating their ideas more effectively in a language that may not be their native tongue ([Krajka & Olszak, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib39); [Lin, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib44)). It is anticipated that the utilization of generative AI would lead to a more equitable distribution of work across co-author teams. AI has the potential to revolutionize data collection by analyzing texts to extract words and meaning. It can also enhance surveys by generating AI-suggested prompts that enable more comprehensive data capture ([Bakla, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib8)). Some qualitative research software currently incorporates AI, and in the near future, with the analysis of human cognition with computer-generated recommendations together. An exploration of the impact of AI on academics' data collection and evaluation processes promises is fascinating.

### 9\. Generative Artificial Intelligence and Research Integrity

In academics, a thorough understanding of the challenges associated with repugnant markets, which refer to exchanges that are seen to violate certain societal standards but nonetheless result in overall benefits, as discussed by [Berg (2023)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib10). It is premature to assert that employing generative AI in research techniques is inherently repugnant.

#### 9.1 Authorial Attribution

The attribution of authorship refers to the official assignment of rights and has multiple key applications in academia. It initially identifies the main author of the text. The author is the individual who advocates for and represents written work ([Bozkurt, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib12)). The assigned author(s) is responsible for protecting the text from criticism and may take legal action if necessary. Under the attribution of authorship, hiding an author's identity is unacceptable. While several academic disciplines have only lately been known, due to security considerations. Until recently, contributions to journalists were predominantly made anonymously ([Raman, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib59)). Presently, it is rare to come across a news story, regardless of its brevity or conciseness that does not include a clearly apparent byline.

Furthermore, asserting authorship entitles an individual to the financial benefits linked to the publication of a piece. According to [Alexander et al. (2023)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib4), it is permissible to assign authorship credit in cases involving extensive research teams, as long as the arrangement and credit are negotiated. The presence of such a compromise highlights the fundamental importance of attribution. However, LLM lacks the ability to regulate the benefits of publishing or assume liability for its content ([Srivastava, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib72)). While authorial credit may be limited to a specific individual or group, it is not something that is restricted or inaccessible. The purpose of is to enable authors to receive recognition for their work and to prevent unauthorized individuals from claiming credit ([Bakla, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib8)). It is important to note that this is not meant to be a critique of those who are not credited. Ultimately, with the growing integration of LLMs into word processing software (Microsoft has already announced its plans to incorporate ChatGPT into its entire office suite, including Microsoft Word), authors will face greater challenges in discerning between manually written content and content generated with the assistance of AI. Writers have derived advantages from tools such as thesauruses, spell checks, grammar checkers (including advanced AI grammar checkers like Grammarly), and currently, predictive text that is incorporated into word processors ([Li et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib42)). Although a new standard attempting to differentiate between technologically assisted and humanly produced content may be beneficial, it is unlikely to gain widespread acceptance. Researchers should not claim credit for work they have not physically and personally typed or written themselves. This further strengthens the argument against utilizing LLMs for writing. Above all, the academy no longer adheres to this ideal. Authorship attribution is a customary practice in teams and laboratories, and it does not require formal communication. A more compelling argument would be to assert that in this instance, the generative AI process of generating ideas is being infringed upon, rather than the generative AI act of writing. An LLM program responds to user interaction. It fails to independently explore its latent space. The value of the results will rely on the researcher's expert evaluation, which is also responsible for designing the search. LLMs are not actually “ideas” but rather the results of an investigator's subjective research question. They are considered academic contributions only when they are published in a journal or pass peer review. Consequently, it can be inferred that an academic writer who use Google Bard or ChatGPT fulfills the criteria for academic authorship ([Sison et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib70)).

However, it is often the case that individuals who contributed to the creation of the final document are not given due credit. One common practice is to engage the services of “professional proof-readers” based on recommendations from reviewers; however, their acknowledgment in published works is rarely observed ([Gill & Kaur, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib25)). Typically, formal acknowledgment of peer reviewers is not very common. However, they contribute greater value to a book compared to workshop participants or the mentors of the writers. Although the double-blind peer review approach avoids the purposeful long-term contracting between authors and reviewers, it would be necessary for a mutually advantageous credit exchange ([Bakla, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib8)).

The accuracy of crediting items produced by LLM in the past appears questionable ([Arighi et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib6); [Kenthapadi et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib35); [King et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib37)). An intriguing use of generative AI is its utility as a tool for editing and augmenting written content. In this context, the researcher supplies ChatGPT with paragraphs, and perhaps entire texts, to be reviewed for the purpose of enhancing their readability. It is not practicable to individually cite each AI-assisted alteration in a footnote or author-date format, and it is not clear to readers what utility there would be in doing so ([Duncan & McCulloh, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib21)).

#### 9.2 The Norm Against Plagiarism

Mentioning plagiarism as a potential objection to the utilization of LLMs in research will suffice to dissuade its adoption among more traditional scholars ([Sysoyev, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib76)). Minimizing charges can have a similarly detrimental impact on a researcher as committing plagiarism. But knowing the economic roots of the criterion against plagiarism helps we traverse the intricacies of LLM contributions to scholarship ([Vinchon et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib78)). It can be relatively difficult to define plagiarism formally. Plagiarism is a Latin term derived from the word “kidnapping.” Some literalists argue that this phrase refers to the act of kidnapping ideas. According to [Dhammi and Ul Haq (2016)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib19), plagiarism can be considered a form of kleptomania. This convention is followed in the casual, daily descriptions. O'Connor (2023) highlights that plagiarism is essentially an act of deceit, where one pretends to be the author of someone else's work ([O'Connor, 2014](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib55)). This emphasizes that individuals are the primary victims of plagiarism rather than being mere results of a search query in the vast collection of human knowledge. [Heckler et al. (2013)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib29) argue that it is imperative to provide a more accurate assessment of the detrimental effects of plagiarism. Plagiarism primarily has contextual impacts and is influenced by traditions. The definition of plagiarism varies among different professions and civilizations. [Tran et al. (2018)](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib77) argues that it is implausible to apply the regulations about plagiarism in academia to different businesses or cultures.

The standard against plagiarism is an extrajudicial technique to preserve intellectual property rights ([Singh & Ganapathy, 2018](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib69)). Plagiarism is neither against the law nor grounds for legal action. Copyright protects only text; ideas are not covered by it. Though they are confined to the sanctions imposed by professional groups, the courts have criticized lawyers for plagiarism, calling it “reprehensible” and “utterly unacceptable” ([Masic, 2014](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib47); [Roig, 2010](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib62)). In academia and other sectors, this convention has evolved to defend the authorship economy from opportunistic action that would deny holders of their rights to the complete worth of their work. An LLM's ideas are truly their own and whether the LLM has ever encountered a violation of their property rights. Not only is language created by AI unable of matching (nonexistent) formal or informal criteria of plagiarism, but the use of LLMs in academics does not breach the framework of property rights that the rule against plagiarism attempts to safeguard ([Berg, 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib10); [Bevolo, 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib11)). One major argument against plagiarism is that the plagiarist breaks an intellectual hierarchy. Readers who accept ideas from others are not able to monitor the evolution of such ideas from the outset. Modern neural network AI architecture is well-known for behaving as an opaque “black box” although it can understand the questions and the training data feed ([Kandeel & Eldakak, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib33)).

Scholars are accountable for developing their theories and arguments in a way that both explains and makes clear where they fit within an intellectual tradition and corpus of literature. The scholar's role and task are evident when considering the importance of opacity in neural network architectures ([Gunjan & Zurada, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib28); [Kashyap et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib34); [Wu et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251007.xhtml#bib81)). They can either utilize an LLM as a tool to investigate a specific field of knowledge or employ an LLM as a guide to assist researchers in acquiring proficiency in that particular field of knowledge. The research paper, as the final outcome, must invariably focus on the specific area and the corresponding academic community. This discusses the ethical responsibility that research academics have toward their academic communities.

### 10\. Future Scope of Generative AI in Academic Writing and Ethics

The potential for creative AI applications in the fields of academic research framework, from idea generation to apply ethical practices in publication is significant. As generative AI advances and gains popularity in several fields, its ability to improve ethical considerations and transform academic writing is predicted to grow. Modern AI algorithms that generate tables and data from structured data, suggest grammar and style, and organize unstructured ideas into coherent arguments can improve research and cognitive writing workflows. This strategy enables the collection of important results and facilitates the rapid identification of vast databases, therefore reducing the need for time-consuming literature reviews. Researchers can enhance their time management skills using these tools. These materials facilitate the enhancement of writing, reading, and speaking skills for non-native English speakers. By tailoring learning experiences, creative AI has the capacity to enhance students' academic performance and facilitate language acquisition. Several strategies to accomplish this objective including translating literary texts, enhancing language proficiency, and implementing grammatical alterations. Employing AI algorithms can assist students in improving their essay writing talents, fostering critical thinking abilities, and obtaining essay samples. Although ethical concerns exist, creative AI has the capacity to automate substantial portions of the writing process, particularly in the realm of instructional writing. This may impede the ability to engage in creative and critical thinking. AI may exhibit bias or errors when it excessively depends on incorrect data or methodologies. The ideas of ethics are vital for use in the classroom. Education is one of the most powerful tools for equalizing society; it is also a sovereign space where critical thinking flourishes. Hence, legislation should impute AI with ethical virtues and make those virtues visible to students as well as teachers. For instance, we could have AI methods that are applied side by side with human decisions to rectify any wrongful opportunities or treatment meted out to groups. It is crucial for human academics and AI systems to interact continuously if Socratic AI-supported research is to maintain the value of human intellectual production. This type of research allows individuals to deepen their understanding of complex subjects, to appraise divergent viewpoints, and even to revise their own ideas. The progress of AI will undoubtedly also lead to new research focused on educational institutions, with data analysis and predictive modeling leading the way. For any person to comprehend accurately the intricate acts of pattern recognition, significant data analysis, or complicated cognitive processes, it would be necessary for them to have an intelligence that surpassed our own in these domains and this is clearly not the case with current AI systems. Despite these limitations, however, many people are concerned about what might happen if such systems are used in important decisions, as they could potentially lead to serious mistakes. In response to these concerns, developers are working on ways to ensure that AI can be used responsibly and effectively alongside human decision-makers. The future of ethical AI in academic writing is here. As AI rises, so too does the risk that it will transform the educational environment in which we inhabit. It could make us more efficient if it can do what we current humans cannot conduct investigations with greater efficacy. And because resolution of issues is at the heart of both creative and analytical AI, these new tools, used responsibly, could enable scholars to see (and be wowed by) the transformative power of such a change.

### 11\. Conclusion

The sudden growth of academia offers both dangers and opportunities for generative AI. Yet, overall, this new academic world presents a wonderful chance to establish the correct (sufficiently rigorous and intellectually honest) use of current technological possibilities especially since many of these technologies are now so user-friendly that even ordinary citizens can work with them. The research that happens in the academy must always be pristine from an ethical standpoint; accordingly, using technology in an unethical way is certainly not an opportunity but a danger. And so, by the same token, using technology correctly and I mean “correctly” in an as-ethical-as-possible way offers another good reason for relying on the academy for the generation of knowledge. Maintaining academic integrity in the collaborative and peer evaluation process is crucial. Rigorous peer evaluation is necessary for proper acknowledgment of scholarly endeavors, and this is even more important when using generative AI to perform tasks that are usually done by humans. A willingness to receive feedback and criticism is a must for academics who want to effectively use AI in their work. Research in AI raises ethical concerns because of its impact on society. If you imagine what kind of “society” a computer can construct based on the kind of data it's given, and on the nature of its “learning” you get some idea of what I'm talking about when I refer to the broader consequences of academic research. Finally, adopting a thorough strategy to reduce risks ensures that AI remains a valuable tool for academics and also ensures that we can ethically harness its capabilities for the betterment of society.

## Chapter 8

## Future Trends and Emerging Possibilities in AI

Simarjit Kaur, Shveta Bansal and Aashima Sharma

Chitkara University, India

### Abstract

Data science and artificial intelligence (AI) have become popular in recent decades and boosted innovation in various sectors due to its real-life applications. An enormous amount of data produced in various sectors can be utilized to produce meaningful insights about the data using data analytical tools. Data science deals with understanding the data, transforming raw data into knowledgeable information that can be used for decision-making. AI is one of the emerging fields of computer science that requires human intelligence to perform different functions, such as predictive analysis, recognizing speech, images, vision, and language translation. AI-based systems can also make decisions using data and rules with minimum human intervention. AI techniques are integration of both machine learning (ML) and deep learning paradigms. Numerous applications are leveraging benefits from AI these days in the fields of e-commerce, navigation, healthcare, agriculture, cybersecurity, NLP, Cloud, and Internet of Things. This chapter aims to focus on the recent trends and innovations in data science and AI. Moreover, it also provides through analysis of emerging possibilities in data science and AI. This research offers a significant direction to the researchers through a comprehensive study of AI, highlighting the basic mechanisms to practical applications, from current status to future trends.

*Keywords*: Artificial intelligence; data science; machine learning; deep learning; cloud computing; fog computing

### 1\. Introduction

In this technological era, artificial intelligence (AI) and data science have become essential and impacted several aspects of society ([Shao et al., 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib23)). AI systems perform various automation and administrative tasks in several fields, such as healthcare, industry, manufacturing, grocery shopping, transportation, etc. AI has influenced daily activities by performing various tasks without human intervention during the last decade ([Wakchaure et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib30)). AI is capable of learning and adapting to real-world applications. Data science is an interdisciplinary field that deals with data collection, cleaning, and processing to provide meaningful information. Integrating AI with data science offers data and real-time valuable information in decision-making, recommendation systems, and solving real-life problems ([Buch et al., 2018](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib2)). Organizations and companies aim at innovation and automation with maximum efficiency and productivity by using AI and data science tools to achieve their goals. Due to automation in industries and organizations, a massive amount of data is available for predictive analytics and pattern recognition in several fields ([Masood & Ahmad, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib16)). The availability of large datasets allows the development of intelligent systems based on data science techniques such as machine learning (ML) and deep learning ([Omitaomu & Niu, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib18)). This chapter presents the future trends and emerging possibilities in AI and data science. The first section presents an overview of AI-based approaches and data science techniques in different fields. Second, the technological integration of AI and data science methods and associated challenges have been addressed. The subsequent section illustrates the limitations and future trends in AI and data science techniques.

#### 1.1 An Overview of AI and Data Science Techniques

AI is an ever-evolving field that focuses on developing intelligent systems capable of performing tasks that typically require human intelligence and cognitive abilities ([Lee & Park, 2019](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib14)). Advanced data science algorithms can process large volumes of data to accomplish AI objectives. Several AI techniques can be categorized into three broad classes:

*   Expert systems,
*   ML techniques, and
*   Deep learning techniques.

![image](images/f08-01.jpg)

Fig. 8.1. Classification of Artificial Intelligence Techniques.

The classification of AI techniques has been depicted in [Fig. 8.1](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#fig1), and these techniques are discussed below.

##### 1.1.1 Expert Systems

Expert systems are a subset of AI that exhibits the power of decision-making in AI systems. These systems utilize knowledge-based and set inference rules to solve problems that require human expertise and thinking abilities ([Omitaomu & Niu, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib18)). The key elements of expert systems are the knowledge base, inference engine, user interface, and domain knowledge given by an expert. [Fig. 8.2](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#fig2) represents the working of an expert system where the specialist knowledge database is a primary component. The knowledge base is defined by rules using logical operations and if-then-else statements. The acquired knowledge and the collection of rules have been applied to conclude the given problem. The effectiveness of expert system relies upon the data provided by field experts and stored in the knowledge base ([Sarker, 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib22)). The performance and decision-making capabilities of the expert system improve by storing and collecting a large amount of knowledge base. The following are significant applications of the expert system.

*   Expert systems have been utilized to manufacture and design physical devices such as automobiles and VLSI systems.
*   To develop systems such as blood diagnosis, the reasoning of specific diseases, and detecting inner malfunctioning.
*   Forecasting systems in different domains, such as rain forecasting, weather forecasting, and energy forecasting, are also developed using expert systems.
*   It can be used for planning and scheduling tasks.
*   Monitoring systems are also expert systems applications that are used to compare the observed behavior of the system against the specified data to detect certain malfunctions and leakage in the oil pipelines.
*   To diagnose faults in the manufacturing of computers and vehicles.

![image](images/f08-02.jpg)

Fig. 8.2. Working of Expert System.

##### 1.1.2 Machine Learning Techniques

ML is a sub-field of AI that learns from the dataset to improve the learning capabilities of expert systems ([Górriz et al., 2020](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib9)). ML-based AI techniques are more adaptive and dynamic need not to be developed and programmed explicitly for every new task ([Chen et al., 2008](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib4)). ML based systems can think and learn the given task using the data related specific domain ([Mellit & Kalogirou, 2008](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib17)). ML techniques are classified into supervised, unsupervised learning and reinforcement learning which are discussed as follows.

*   Supervised learning techniques
*   The supervised learning uses the labeled input data to perform classification and prediction tasks. The labeled dataset consists of labeled input and output parameters and the learning algorithm map the input datapoints with output data to get accurate results. There are two types of supervised learning techniques, namely classification and regression. The classification algorithms are known as classifier which is used to classify the category of labeled input using the training dataset and produces yes/no, spam/not spam and 0/1 as output classes. For example, spam detection, disease prediction, image classification, fraud detection, etc. are classification problems. There are two types of classifiers; binary and multi-class classifier. If the problem has two outcomes like yes/no, true/false, and 0/1, then it is known as binary classifier. Multi-class classifier consists of more than two classes for example types of crops, music classification. SVM is a well-known supervised classifier based on statistical learning theory. It has been applied to solve classification as well as regression problems. The SVM algorithm makes a best line or decision boundary to separate n-dimensional space into different classes. SVM has a significant number of applications in the field of energy prediction and forecasting. The classifiers provide high accuracy and work well with high dimensional space using significantly less memory. A supervised learning algorithm, random forest, is commonly used for classification and regression analysis ([Kaur et al., 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib12)). It is an ensemble learning algorithm that combines multiple classifiers to solve a complex problem and improve the model's performance. The primary focus of random forest is to deal with a large number of values.
*   Decision trees are used for classification and prediction problems. It is flowchart like tree structure that learn from dataset. Decision tree is a classifier in the form of tree which consists of a leaf node and decision node. The leaf node is the target attribute or the outcome of the test and decision node perform some test on the attribute value. K-Nearest Neighbors (K-NN) is a classification algorithm initially used for pattern recognition and statistical analysis as a non-parametric technique. The input consists of K-closest data values which forms a cluster or group. When an unclassified value occurs, it is assigned to a group with close nearest neighbor.
*   Unsupervised learning techniques
*   Unsupervised learning techniques are useful to extract hidden patterns and deduce significant information from the unlabeled data. The commonly utilized unsupervised learning techniques are clustering and dimensionality reduction. The most widely used clustering techniques are K-means, hierarchical clustering, and DBSCAN (Density-Based Spatial Clustering of Applications with Noise), which are used for forecasting and classification tasks. K-means is a distance based or centroid based statistical clustering algorithm in which *k* number of centroids are identified, and each data point is allocated to the nearest cluster. Hierarchical clustering approach creates the clusters by calculating the dissimilarities among data points. It constructs a tree in a top-down (divisive) and bottom-up (agglomerative) manner. DBSCAN is a density-based clustering algorithm used to identify noise in the data by highlighting the denser regions. Dimensionality reduction is an unsupervised learning approach has been used to transform the high dimensional space data to a lower dimension and eliminate redundant features. It has been applied to process large datasets efficiently. Principal component analysis (PCA) and linear discriminant analysis (LDA) are the most popular dimensionality reduction algorithms. PCA applies linear transformation and creates a new dimension to reduce the dimensionality of data. It is used as a preprocessing method while training ML models. LDA preserve the crucial information while reducing the dimensionality of data. LDA can be used to solve multi-class classification problems and design face detection algorithms and disease classification problems.

##### 1.1.3 Deep Learning Techniques

Deep learning is a branch of AI based on the concept on several layers of neural networks. A well-trained neural network model performs very fast as compared to other AI models. Deep learning can perform complex tasks on large datasets and real-world AI applications in various sectors, including energy optimization, automobile, healthcare, manufacturing, etc ([Fan et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib6)). Various deep learning approaches such as artificial neural networks (ANN), convolutional neural networks (CNN), long short-term memory (LSTM), and autoencoders have been used by researchers to solve complex real-world problems. ANNs are computing networks inspired by biological neural networks. An ANN algorithm has the ability to learn from non-linear datasets and fluctuating data values ([Mellit & Kalogirou, 2008](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib17)). ANN consists of three layers: input, hidden, and output. In recent research, the ANN algorithm has been used for predictive analytics, character recognition, anomaly detection, etc. CNN has been widely adopted for image classification, processing satellite images and solving character recognition problems. It consists of several convolutional and sub-sampling layers to process the data that pass through several layers to classify input features. A supervised learning approach multilayer perceptron (MLP) belongs to class of ANN, which converts a set of input values to output value. MLP comprises three layers: input layer, hidden layer, and output layer. There can be one or more non-linear hidden layers ([Mellit & Kalogirou, 2008](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib17)). The perceptron has multiple layers connected using directed graphs. LSTMs is a special type recurrent neural networks (RNN) that can learn from long-term dependencies ([Kaur et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib13)). Its sequential network makes it suitable for time-sequence data prediction, processing text, and speech data. The LSTM comprises various cells, each consisting of input, output and forget gate, which control the flow of information going into and coming out of the cell. It also has a memory cell that keeps the information of previous time steps, contributing to the current cell's output. Autoencoder is a self-supervised learning model widely used in data processing tasks, denoising, feature extraction, and dimensionality reduction. Autoencoders are used to map data to lower dimensional space and transform it back to the original data.

### 2\. Emerging Applications of AI

AI, whose astounding advances in numerous fields and applications have fundamentally altered everyday life over the past few years, is an important field of study for academia and business. However, AI is still in its early stages of study, theory, research, methodology, and applications.

![image](images/f08-03.jpg)

Fig. 8.3. Innovative AI Applications.

However, after the invention of AI in 1956, it gained a wide range of interest and reputation, and the idea of AI evolved and is still elaborated ([Shao et al., 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib23)). Over the previous several decades, computer science, healthcare, mathematics, psychology, industries, linguistics, and many other fields have been included in AI. As depicted in [Fig. 8.3](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#fig3), some major innovative applications in which AI is gaining rapid interest have been discussed below.

#### 2.1 AI in Healthcare and Remote Medical Procedures

The core of evidence-based medicine is using historical data to inform clinical decision-making ([Rana & Shuford, 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib21)). Traditionally, statistical techniques have tackled this task by using mathematical equations to characterize patterns in the data. With ML, AI offers methods for locating intricate relationships that are difficult to solve mathematically ([Buch et al., 2018](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib2)). For instance, neural networks, like the human brain, represent data through enormous numbers of interconnected neurons. This enables ML systems to tackle complicated issue-solving like that of a clinician by carefully analyzing the data to arrive at well-reasoned conclusions. However, unlike a single doctor, these systems can quickly and concurrently observe an almost infinite amount of inputs. With the advent of wireless and robotic technology, remote surgery can also be made possible. Remote or robotic surgeries transform surgical practice virtually by bringing surgeons and patients together who live at different remote locations ([Sharma et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib24)). Using 6G and AI technology, this novel strategy uses robotics and cutting-edge telecommunications. The ultra-fast data transfer speed and flawless connection among remote surgical teams experienced doctors, and concerned patients overcome the need for physical treatments and the availability of top-notch doctors even in low-boundary areas. With the support of AI-enabled algorithms, telesurgery robots can adapt and enhance surgical results with accuracy and agility ([Buch et al., 2018](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib2)). Also, real-time data analysis and processing capabilities of AI algorithms improve the success rate of medical procedures and patient safety as they empower remote doctors to make well-informed decisions. New technologies entering the medical profession must combine with present procedures, follow the necessary authorization rules, and motivate patients and medical professionals to adopt a new way of thinking ([Chataut et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib3)). These difficulties have led to several new developments in AI use and research. The collaboration between AI, ML, and 6G technologies provides more accurate, predictive and personalized medical services and promises to promote patient care.

#### 2.2 Intelligent Grids With AI and 6G

The creation and operation of smart grids are undergoing a major upheaval due to the integration of AI and 6G ([Zuo et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib31)). It is projected that 6G networks and AI will provide extremely high data transfer rates, extremely low latency, and wide-ranging device connectivity, significantly enhancing smart grids' communication infrastructure. This will allow for effective energy distribution, management, and consumption and the ability to respond quickly to fluctuations by enabling real-time supervision, control, and energy saving. When combined with 6G networks, AI technologies expand the potential of smart grids by facilitating autonomous decision-making, predictive modeling, and advanced analytics ([Chataut et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib3)). AI algorithms can find trends, analyze the problems and improve the performance of grids as they can gather and store vast amounts of data from the sensors, meters and other devices. The smart grids have been revolutionized by the imminent features, competency and durability of AI technology, opening the door for an enhanced autonomous and sustainable energy environment ([Qiao et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib20)).

#### 2.3 AI in Brain–Computer Interfaces (BCI)

Brain–computer interfaces can create a bridge between the brain and computer in order to control the devices with the thoughts of the human. Compared with traditional input devices such as a mouse or keyboard, a BC interface transforms the brain signals into computer-based control commands. These interfaces focus on enabling well-equipped facilities such as smart wheelchairs or prosthetic limbs. AI networks will provide high-speed data transmission with minimum latency to enable and process real-time brain catalysts. Some emerging technologies like edge, fog, and terahertz communication will use AI programs in the future to develop portable and sophisticated BCIs, enhanced performance, high computing, and processor capabilities ([Chataut et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib3)). Additionally, with the day-to-day advancement of AI programs, more and more research possibilities will arise for creating and deploying customized BCI applications.

#### 2.4 Blockchain With AI and 6G

A decentralized technology that makes it easy to record transparent and safe digital transactions is known as blockchain. Integrating blockchain with AI provides numerous benefits and potential uses to the users. 6G, along with AI, with its anticipated increased network capacity, decreased latency, and improved data transfer rates, could enable the processing of complicated transactions and applications in real time ([Shao et al., 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib23)). Blockchain technology, an innovation, or a business intelligence program can be used to trace device history. Block bodies and headers are related strings that make up a blockchain. All of the block's transactions make up the block body. However, the hash value is used to build the block header, a Merkle root of the transactions in the preceding block and its timestamp. As a result, each block is generated and connected to the others using the hash value of the preceding block. Any block interfered with will alter its hash value, impacting all other blocks. This indicates that blockchain technology will be available to safeguard data integrity. Aside from cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin, blockchain technology has been utilized to improve and optimize already-existing systems like cloud storage, authentication, healthcare, and more ([Gill et al., 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib8)). The device's credibility will rise due to the integration of blockchain, which enables a transparent audit trail of the data and models used to validate the machine's decision process. In terms of machine-to-machine communication, the latter is crucial. Furthermore, by reducing the action space, applying AI code over Decentralized Autonomous Organizations (DAOs) with smart contracts connected reduces catastrophic risk situations.

Enhancing communication and transaction security and privacy is one possible application of blockchain technology in 6G and AI. The high throughput and speed provided by AI and 6G improve data security and defend against hacks and data breaches in blockchain-based systems ([Qiao et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib20)). It can also analyze and store vast amounts of sensitive information more securely, such as records of financial transactions or medical reports and prescriptions. Although the blockchain with AI is still in its early stages of development, it has a wide range of potential uses for the stakeholders.

#### 2.5 Space Exploration With AI

The development of AI technology could make real-time communication easier, which directly impacts space missions. AI facilitates quick decision-making and simplifies missions between spacecraft and ground control. This also will produce more precise and thorough information for finding new scientific possibilities, guaranteeing reliable and continuous connection. Also, AIs with high-resolution virtual and augmented reality will provide increased network capacity and reduced latency, providing more captivating opportunities for space exploration researchers ([Qiao et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib20)).

#### 2.6 AI in Augmented Reality (AR)

The seamless integration of the virtual and physical worlds is made possible by AI technology to provide real-time capabilities in mixed and augmented reality (MAR). In AR applications, the enhancement of visual and sensory aids can be achieved by transmitting high-resolution virtual and augmented reality data ([Dwivedi et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib5)). Integrating 6G and AI creates new opportunities for product visualization, entertainment, and education.

Furthermore, AI can create virtual links with people who are remotely located for social interaction, gaming, and remote connections. The main purpose of using AI with MAR is to protect private and confidential data with end-to-end security features ([Chataut et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib3)).

#### 2.7 Internet of Things (IoT) and AI

IoT provides significant opportunities and transformation to industries when combined with AI, i.e., the Industrial Internet of Things (IIoT) ([Verma & Bala, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib29)). This integration results in effective and flexible real-time industry communication ([Gill et al., 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib8)). With AI's high processing speed and power capabilities, IoT applications can improve decision-making accuracy and provide more insight analysis of large amounts of data collected from sensors and other devices. Moreover, industrial environments can provide stronger protection against cyber threats and data loss while using the sophisticated security features in AI-based IoT applications. The latest emerging IoT-based solutions, such as remote industrial system, control and prediction system, may become possible by integrating AI and IIoT ([Chataut et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib3)). AI can potentially reduce costs by accelerating the development of automated and intelligent industrial systems.

#### 2.8 AI in Robotics

The application of AI in robotics is anticipated to be substantial and impactful because of the enhanced capabilities and better connectivity of 6G networks. The real-time control and communication between autonomous robots and drones through AI algorithms will improve their speed and accuracy. New applications such as intelligent homes, smart industries, and autonomous vehicles will be enabled with advanced AI-powered systems that will only work in sophistication ([Shao et al., 2022](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib23)). Moreover, better natural language processing capabilities will open many doors for virtual assistants. In industrial settings, AI will be utilized for prediction maintenance and monitoring. Also, AI integration with 6G networks will enable the prediction, management and monitoring of robotic equipment remotely in unexpected, dangerous conditions ([Chataut et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib3)).

#### 2.9 AI-Enabled Autonomous Vehicles and Intelligent Transportation Systems

AI technology can be used for autonomous cars and intelligent transportation systems, providing significant breakthroughs and enhancements. AI technology can provide the infrastructure required for driverless cars to operate safely and effectively, enabling real-time control and communication between vehicles, the centralized traffic control system, and the underlying infrastructure. Adopting AI and 6G will enhance the dependability and safety of autonomous vehicles by enabling quick and more accurate decision-making capabilities ([Chataut et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib3)). The traffic flow and management will also be optimized by real-time data interchange between infrastructure and AI-enabled vehicles, boosting productivity and lowering congestion ([Aldakkhelallah & Simic, 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib1)). The intelligent transport systems will also deploy current autonomous vehicle technologies like sensors and mapping with AI's enhanced features and connectivity.

### 3\. Future Trends and Open Challenges in AI

AI has been widely discussed in public discourse for many years. It is typically portrayed in fiction movies, and debates are being made regarding the potential for intelligent computers to dominate the planet, reducing humans to a monotonous and subservient existence in service of the new AI regime. Although this image may resemble a caricature, it is essential to acknowledge that AI is already a reality today. AI has recently expanded due to its vast scope of applications in the real world, such as healthcare, robotics, face recognition, industrial applications, data mining, and government practices on a global scale. Identifying research possibilities for utilizing AI and the upcoming technologies for data science in intelligent computing for upcoming paradigms such as blockchain ([Sharma et al., 2024](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib25)), IoT, edge, quantum computing, cloud, fog, and serverless environments is crucial, as illustrated in [Fig. 8.4](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#fig4). This section presents the associated challenges in an AI-integrated computing environment.

![image](images/f08-04.jpg)

Fig. 8.4. Next-Generation AI Computing Paradigms.

#### 3.1 Cloud Computing

The correlation between the growth of cloud computing and the advancement of AI is becoming more apparent. Integrating AI with the cloud can enhance modern infrastructure's functionality, effectiveness, and digital growth ([Pusztai et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib19)). Integrating AI into the cloud computing environment is essential for organizations to enhance efficiency, strategic decision-making, and data-driven insights. Additionally, it offers improved adaptability, flexibility, and system responsiveness. Cloud computing environments can be optimized by integrating AI and cloud computing, enhancing such environments' strategic planning and insightfulness. The real-life applications using cloud computing and AI are specified in [Table 8.1](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#tbl1).

##### 3.1.1 Open Challenges in Implementing Cloud Computing With AI in Data Science

*Distributed and Parallel Computing:* AI and data science tasks necessitate distributed computing, such as training complex neural networks or handling enormous datasets. Continuously addressing the persistent challenge of effectively distributing these workloads among different cloud instances, with minimal communication overhead and providing fault tolerance will prove very helpful.

*Scalability and Elasticity:* Given the increasing scale and complexities of AI algorithms and datasets, it is essential to prioritize the ability to scale and flexibility of cloud computing resources. Effectively managing and coordinating the distribution of storage and computational resources among several cloud area networks or providers is a continuous and difficult task.

*Security and Privacy:* It is essential to prioritize data privacy and legal compliance while hosting sensitive data and AI algorithms on the cloud. Research focuses on implementing access control mechanisms and strong encryption approaches for cloud data science and AI applications.

#### 3.2 Fog Computing

Fog computing was introduced as an alternative to cloud computing services to meet the increasing utilization of the IoT and the need to manage and store large volumes of generated data ([Singh et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib26)). Fog computing can provide essential network services to assist IoT applications that have reduced response time demands ([Gubbi et al., 2013](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib10)). Distributing IoT application activities among fog nodes for meeting quality of service and quality of experience limits is challenging because of fog nodes distributed, diverse, and limited resources ([Mahmud et al., 2019](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib15)). Fog computing is utilized in Vehicle-to-Everything (V2X), patient monitoring, and automation in industries because it offers computing capabilities near the user. This ensures that the applications can fulfill the expected reaction time. Consequently, the widespread utilization of IoT devices leads to the generation of vast quantities of data by these applications. Cloud computing fails to meet latency requirements ([Singh et al., 2021](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib26)) due to delays in distant data transfer and network congestion. Fog computing expands cloud computing ([Gill et al., 2020](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib7)) due to its reduced bandwidth, efficient energy management, and low latency requirements for data delivery. Hence, the data produced by these IoT devices becomes useful for various applications. Researchers now use AI, deep learning, and ML more frequently to deal with this massive amount of data for data analysis and decision help ([Iftikhar et al., 2023](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#bib11)). AI powers millions of sensors and devices that operate independently from the data center to the network's edge. Specifically, edge computing and fog computing are the main technologies used when it comes to data processing, data analysis, and decision-making. AI models on fog nodes can be employed to perform real-time data analysis that can help provide better insights about the data, further optimizing the operations. Emerging frameworks have arisen from the seamless amalgamation of sensor data and AI. Emerging concepts such as “Smart home” are being utilized to describe innovative technologies that enhance the ease and convenience of managing various facets of everyday activities, including energy consumption within our households. For instance, we can store sensor data and take action based on it, streamline operations using AI, and respond immediately to situations that previously required direct human intervention. AI models deployed on fog computing can identify anomalies, predict potential setbacks, and recommend necessary actions. [Table 8.2](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#tbl2) highlights some of the real-life fog computing applications using AI.

##### 3.2.1 Open Challenges in Implementing Fog Computing With AI in Data Science

*Edge Intelligence and Federated Learning:* Edge devices generate and analyze data in many fog computing environments. It is one of the key fields in current research; more exactly, it intends to deploy effective methods of edge computing, such as federated learning. This technique enables AI model training on edge-based distributed data without collecting data at a central location. As such, research areas on integrating fog and edge computing for federated learning remain very active. Mechanisms of managing the allocations of tasks at different layers, sharing of data, and updates of models that reduce unnecessary communication while at the same time maintaining uniformity are very challenging tasks.

*Minimizing latency for model partitioning and adaption:* It is very difficult to ensure real-time AI applications are quick and responsive regarding synchronization and analysis in a dispersed fog setting. To improve the performance of AI models on fog nodes, the models can be split into smaller pieces, which may involve distributing the model across devices with different capacities. This is an area of focus for fast-tracking data streams, possibly necessitating task assignment among fog nodes. Modifying and enhancing AI models to run on fog node's hardware and software configurations may be problematic.

#### 3.3 Edge Computing

The amalgamation of edge computing and AI is a powerful combination to address the problems associated with the traditional cloud-centric approaches now known as Edge AI. Edge computing is crucial in facilitating AI by providing superior performance at a reduced expense. Edge computing addresses the technological challenges faced by applications powered by AI due to their information and computation-intensive nature. This leads to optimization, faster response times, and enhanced operational efficiency. The big data requirements for Edge AI are specified in [Table 8.3](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#tbl3). Another application of Edge AI is predictive maintenance, where data processing can be done to anticipate any significant failures or risks. Edge computing enables high-performance business services by leveraging local processing power, data analytics capabilities, and devices close to the end users. Industrial automated processes, urban planning, personalized medical care, and autonomous vehicles are just a few areas that can benefit from the enhanced performance, real-time responsiveness, cost-effective deployment, and decreased latency of AI solutions. ##### 3.3.1 Open Challenges in Implementing Edge Computing With AI in Data Science

*Balance between optimality and efficiency:* While AI can certainly offer optimal solutions, it is important to consider the relationship between efficiency and optimality, especially in resource-constrained edge environments. Therefore, enhancing the user-friendliness and effectiveness of edge computing platforms for different applications that use algorithms is a significant problem.

*Model Establishment:* It is necessary to impose limitations on the computational models and constrain the mathematical formulations to utilize AI techniques. One reason for this is that the techniques for optimization used in AI are not effective when the search space is limited. However, particularly for Markov Decision Processes (MDPs), it is impossible for the state set and action set to be infinite. A conventional approach involves converting the limitations into a penalty and integrating them into the overarching objective of global optimization. The current state of affairs severely limits the creation of mathematical models, resulting in a decline in performance. It can be seen as an acceptable compromise for applying AI techniques. Consequently, determining how to create a suitable system model presents significant difficulties.

*Algorithm Deployment:* A limited number of studies can attain an accurate and approximation ideal solution using convex optimization methods. Primarily, regarding AI for edge computing, the answer predominantly relies on iteration learning-based methods. When implementing these technologies on the edge in an actual scenario, numerous difficulties arise. In addition, another overlooked problem pertains to determining the appropriate edge device that should assume the task of delivering and executing the complex algorithms that have been proposed. Existing research efforts typically focus on their specific challenges and do not provide detailed information.

#### 3.4 Quantum Computing

Major technology giants such as IBM, Google, and Intel have demonstrated a significant interest in quantum computing in recent years. Concurrently, leading quantum computing companies, including IonQ and D-Wave, have consistently unveiled their progress in developing quantum software kits and hardware advancements. In contrast to traditional technologies, quantum computing has demonstrated the potential to substantially enhance the efficiency of certain tasks, making these cutting-edge technologies the focus of heightened interest and attention. Quantum computing, specifically within the realm of data science with AI, can mutually enhance and support one another through various means. The real-life applications using quantum computing and AI are specified in [Table 8.5](#978-1-83608-298-920251008.xhtml#tbl5). Below are a few possible uses and advantages of integrating quantum computing with AI in the field of data science:

Data Optimization: Quantum computing can greatly aid some optimization issues in data science, such as resource allocation, portfolio, and combinatorial optimization, by effectively exploring a wide range of potential solutions. Quantum methods, such as Grover's algorithm and quantum annealing, can discover the most effective approaches more rapidly than conventional algorithms for specific problem categories.

Data Modeling and Simulation: Quantum computing allows for improved modeling and comprehension of a wide range of processes beyond the capabilities of conventional computers. Such developments could potentially yield valuable knowledge and progress in disciplines such as enhancing the efficacy of ML and deep learning models on complex datasets, all of which significantly depend on the interpretation of data.

Quantum-Inspired AI: Researchers are currently investigating quantum-based AI algorithms for conventional computers, even in the absence of large-scale quantum computers. These algorithms utilize principles from quantum walks and quantum annealing algorithms to address optimizing and sampling challenges in data science.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2. || JPMorgan Chase and IBM || Financial Services || JPMorgan Chase works with IBM to develop quantum computing solutions for complex financial problems like option pricing and fraud detection. |
| 3. || Google's Quantum AI || ML || Google is developing quantum ML algorithms to improve the training and performance of AI models, potentially leading to faster and more accurate AI systems. |
| 4. || Microsoft and 1QBit || Material Science || In collaboration with 1QBit, Microsoft uses quantum computing and AI to design new materials with specific properties, which is crucial for energy storage and electronics advancements. |
| 5. || ExxonMobil and IBM || Energy || ExxonMobil partners with IBM to apply quantum computing and AI to optimize chemical reactions and energy production processes, potentially leading to more efficient fuel production. |

##### 3.4.1 Open Challenges in Implementing Edge Computing With AI in Data Science

*Quantum Algorithm Development:* Certain issues can be solved using quantum computers. Yet creating quantum algorithms for intelligence tasks such as optimizing resources, ML, and analyzing data remains an active field of study. Adapting AI models to work in a quantum framework poses a challenge as it requires the design of new algorithms customized for quantum computing.

*Hardware Scalability:* At present, quantum systems have a limited number of qubits and short periods of coherence, i.e., the time qubits remain in a stable quantum state. Increasing the scale of quantum hardware to support large-scale AI deployments is a significant engineering challenge.

*Quantum Fault tolerance:* The presence of noise has an impact, on qubits potentially disrupting quantum operations. It is crucial to advance quantum error correction and fault tolerant strategies to ensure the execution of practical AI tasks on quantum systems.

### 4\. Limitations and Future Research Directions

The state-of-the-art provides a brief insight to current applications of AI. AI is a broad category that includes many different technologies and benefits. The tendencies noted throughout the survey may soon become outdated as AI develops rapidly. In few months or years, what appears to be a trend today might not hold. The survey may lack or restrict to provide a limited or partial picture of complex AI domains. Moreover, this work may not adequately address the complex ethical and social issues surrounding the future of AI. The future research in this area can benefit from the insights provided by this work regarding how AI can support current application areas. The results of this work can be used to examine the applications of AI incorporated into current systems to enhance performance and innovation. By outlining recent developments in AI, a framework for future research might get direct guidance to optimize these technologies for diverse applications.

### 5\. Conclusion

This chapter provides in-depth significance and analyses of the wide-ranging role of AI in the next-generation technologies and applications. Numerous cutting-edge applications and industries will benefit from AI's broad and distinctive uses, such as autonomous systems, brain–computer interfaces, smart cities, healthcare, and many more. Moreover, the integration of AI and 6G networks results in transforming network management and user experience. By deploying the prediction and adaption capabilities of AI, these networks will be able to provide more personalized and autonomous services, optimized bandwidth, and increased efficiency. To conclude, this chapter provides comprehensive reviews on emerging AI technology, highlighting the novel path for more research and development. As the world is heading toward better intelligent technologies, the research in the field of network and wireless communication will going to be greatly influenced by the speculation presented here in the future.
